Workers who paid middlemen with no written agreement have only their testimony, passport and BMET card to show <sup>[N94 (2024)](#n94)</sup>. A complaint proved against a registered sub-agent can cost him his registration <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>.

2. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-2"></a>**If BMET does not settle it,** go up the ladder.

   You'll need <sup>[48 (2024)](#o48), [1](#o1)</sup>:

   - your complaint ID;
   - copies of everything you gave BMET.

   1. Write to BMET's Additional Director General (Employment), adgemp@bmet.gov.bd, 02-48321949 or 01720105492 <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   2. Then write to the Ministry's Secretary, secretary@probashi.gov.bd, who has 1 month <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   3. Then file on the Cabinet Division's Grievance Redress System at [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/), which has 3 months <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

3. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-3"></a>**Or complain straight to the Ministry.** It is free; action starts within 3 to 5 working days, both sides are heard, and it is meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>:

   - an application;
   - documents proving the complaint;
   - your full address;
   - the agency's RL number and address.

   Send them to the Ministry's Enforcement-2 branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255, or to the Ministry at Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000 <sup>[100](#o100), [101](#o101)</sup>.

   The branch also holds mobile courts (on-the-spot courts under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013) on its own initiative, free, within 7 working days, giving immediate remedies to people cheated before or after going <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>. For advice, call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

4. <a id="if-an-agency-or-broker-takes-your-money-item-4"></a>**Courts and NGOs.** You can go to court alongside a BMET complaint, but many workers can't keep a case going without money or legal aid <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>; with NGO legal help, some have sued agents or brokers under the 2013 Act <sup>[N94 (2024)](#n94)</sup>, which since a 2024 amendment also covers sub-agents and other intermediaries <sup>[N131](#n131)</sup>.

   You'll need: the same papers as for the BMET complaint <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.

   - Free legal advice: the government legal aid helpline, 16699 (+8809612316699 from abroad), run by NLASO (National Legal Aid Services Organization), which has a legal aid office in each district <sup>[102](#o102), [103](#o103)</sup>.
   - A village court can order money owed under a contract, receipt or other document repaid, up to Tk 3 lakh <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.
   - Migrant NGOs such as WARBE (Welfare Association for the Rights of Bangladeshi Emigrants) and the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum settle some disputes by village mediation and pass agency cases to BMET <sup>[N94 (2024)](#n94)</sup>; WARBE does not take fraud by local sub-agents <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which court and case type does a worker use against an agency or broker?
   > - What does a court case against an agency or broker cost?
   > - Where does a worker file at the village court?
   > - What fee does the village court charge?
   > - How does a worker contact WARBE?
   > - How does a worker contact the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum?

<a id="step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical"></a>

### 3.3 Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical)

Every worker must pass a medical check (the "Bio-Medical") at a Bangladesh centre accredited by FWCMS before the employer can apply for the visa in [Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. A medical done before 31 October 2026 is not accepted for immigration clearance <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-medical-check-in-bangladesh-bio-medical-item-1"></a>**Get the medical slip from your agency and test at an approved centre.** Your agency makes your MiGRAMS slip; no fee may be charged for it <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>. The agency gives the centre your papers and VDR visa papers ([Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[8](#o8), [31](#o31)</sup>:

   - the medical slip and papers from your agency;
   - Tk 8,000, the fee for general workers.

   1. Find a centre at [fwcms.com.my/affiliates](https://fwcms.com.my/affiliates/), under Accredited Medical Centres, Bangladesh (name and address of each) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. You may use any centre on the list, not only the one the agency names <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 8,000, no more, and keep the receipt for any complaint <sup>[31](#o31), [8](#o8)</sup>.
   3. If you pass, the employer can apply for your visa <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>. If you move to another agency, the first must send your medical report on without delay <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which comes first, the medical (FWCMS requires it before the VDR application <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>) or the VDR (the Ministry requires VDR papers at the medical <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>)?
   > - Does the centre ask for your passport?
   > - How do you check a MiGRAMS slip is real?

   **Check it is real and scams:** brokers and dishonest agencies offer unauthorised medicals to get passports and money, and have charged for medicals for jobs that never came <sup>[8](#o8), [N62](#n62)</sup>. Hand over nothing until the centre is confirmed on the FWCMS list, and test only after the agency gives you a MiGRAMS slip for a real job <sup>[8](#o8), [26](#o26), [31](#o31)</sup>. The list is not enough: Human Diagnostic and Medical Centre, Box Culvert Road, Paltan, Dhaka, was still on it in November 2026 after a mobile court sealed it on 1 October for testing Malaysia-bound workers without approval <sup>[26](#o26), [N132](#n132), [N133](#n133)</sup>. A centre that breaks the Ministry's instructions loses its approval <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where can a worker check that an FWCMS-listed centre is also approved by the Ministry?

   **If a centre overcharges, harasses you or tests without approval, or a broker took your passport or money for a medical:**

   1. Email the Ministry at secretary@probashi.gov.bd with the date, place, names of the people or centre, and evidence (receipt, photos, messages) <sup>[8](#o8), [31](#o31)</sup>. For example: "To the Secretary, Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment. On [date], [centre, address] charged me Tk [amount] for my Malaysia medical, against the fixed Tk 8,000. My agency is [name, RL number]. Receipt attached. Please take action and return the extra money. [Name, phone, NID]." <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>
   2. Send the same to the Ministry's enforcement branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255, with your full address and the agency's RL number and address; free, action within 3 to 5 working days, settled within 30, and it also runs mobile courts ([Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", route 3) <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.
   3. If an agency is involved, also complain to BMET as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money" <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
   4. If a broker kept your passport, file an online GD (general diary) at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.
   5. Call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr"></a>

### 3.4 Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)

1. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-1"></a>**The employer applies; you wait in Bangladesh.** After quota approval and paying the levy, the employer applies online through FWCMS for your VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, visa with reference; FWCMS calls it the Calling Visa) with the Home Ministry approval letter, levy receipt, a security deposit or guarantee, and your passport copy and photo; you stay in Bangladesh until it is approved <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5), [23](#o23)</sup>. The deposit, visa fee, pass and levy are the employer's costs, but some deduct the levy from wages, often secretly <sup>[9](#o9), [N73](#n73)</sup>.

   **If the levy is taken from your wages,** use the routes under If you aren't paid:

   - the Labour Department's Working for Workers (WFW) app, 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106), [97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>;
   - the High Commission's labour wing <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>;
   - the migrant call centre, 16135 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2"></a>**Track the approval.** You'll need the employer's company registration number (or an individual employer's Malaysian identity card number) and the application number <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. On IMI's [MyIMMs eServices](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main?pra) choose "Status Permohonan Pembantu Rumah Asing / Pekerja Asing" and enter both <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>:

   - BARU: processing;
   - LULUS: approved;
   - BAYAR: paid;
   - CETAK: visa sticker printed;
   - TOLAK: rejected;
   - BATAL: cancelled;
   - TANGGUH: deferred.

   A complete eVDR (online VDR) should be approved within 5 working days for Peninsular Malaysia, 14 for Sabah, 30 for Sarawak <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>.

   **If the VDR is refused:** an agency that fails to send workers gets no further Ministry permissions until it repays them <sup>[37 (2024)](#o37)</sup>; claim your money back through BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money" <sup>[1](#o1), [100](#o100)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-3"></a>**The entry visa is issued in Dhaka.** After IMI approves, the High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka issues the VDR <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Worker visa applications go to its authorised visa application agencies, not to the High Commission itself <sup>[61](#o61), [51](#o51)</sup>. The Tk 600 visa fee is the employer's; an RM105 (about Tk 2,500) processing fee and the agency's own charge also apply <sup>[51](#o51), [9](#o9), [61](#o61)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who gives the worker the VDR application number?
   > - Who pays the processing fee and agency charge?
   > - Which agencies handle worker visas?

   To ask, contact the visa section: dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918, House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave, Dhaka, Sunday to Thursday, 8.30 to 4.30 <sup>[61](#o61), [108](#o108)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.5 Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

1. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get an e-passport valid long enough:** at least 18 to 24 months left <sup>[14](#o14)</sup>; the work pass can be renewed only while your passport has more than 12 months left <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>:

   - your original NID (compulsory over age 20);
   - for some jobs, technical certificates such as a driving licence.

   1. On [epassport.gov.bd](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/) choose your present-address district and police station to get your Regional Passport Office, and fill in the form exactly as on your NID; nothing needs attesting <sup>[110](#o110), [109](#o109)</sup>. A Bangladeshi abroad can correct the age on a reissued passport by at most 8 years, on the basis of the birth registration <sup>[N134](#n134)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Do brokers alter ages or names on passports for Malaysia jobs?
      > - What does an altered age or name do to PLKS renewal?
      > - What does an altered age or name do to EPF withdrawal?

   2. Pay only through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay (card, bKash, Nagad or Rocket), or at a bank through A-Challan <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>. A 48-page passport costs <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>:

      - 5 years, regular: Tk 4,025;
      - 5 years, express: Tk 6,325;
      - 5 years, super express: Tk 8,625;
      - 10 years, regular: Tk 5,750;
      - 10 years, express: Tk 8,050;
      - 10 years, super express: Tk 10,350.

   3. Enrol (photo and fingerprints) at that office with the printed appointment summary and form, your original NID, and the bank slip if you paid at a bank <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.
   4. With an online-verified NID there is no police verification; delivery takes up to 15, 7 or 2 working days from enrolment (regular, express, super express) <sup>[113](#o113), [111](#o111)</sup>.

   **To complain:**

   1. Passport helpline 16445 (09666716445 from abroad, 24 hours) <sup>[114 (2023)](#o114)</sup>.
   2. Then the office's grievance redress officer, listed on its website (for example the Dhaka Secretariat office: 01538855234, rpo_secretariat@passport.gov.bd) <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>.
   3. Then [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/) <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>.

   **If it is lost or stolen:**

   1. File a GD quickly at the nearest police station <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>, or at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) or the Online GD app with your NID number, a mobile number (for an SMS code) and a live photo, entering what was lost, the district, the police station, and the time and place <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>. You get a digital GD copy with its number and the investigating officer's details (help: gd@police.gov.bd, 01320001428 on WhatsApp) <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>.
   2. Re-apply with copies of the old passport and the GD <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Police clearance only if asked;** it is not on IMI's VDR list or BMET's clearance lists <sup>[9](#o9), [29](#o29), [1](#o1)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Malaysian employers or agencies still ask for a police clearance?

   If asked, you'll need <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>:

   - a passport valid at least 3 months;
   - a present address that is one of your passport's addresses;
   - a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer.

   1. Apply at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/) <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.
   3. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting PCC S and your reference number to 26969 <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.
   4. Collect it with a copy of your passport or application, or, if you are abroad, through someone with an attested authorisation letter giving their name and NID number <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.

   Helpdesk: Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>.

<a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself"></a>

### 3.6 Step 5: Check the visa yourself

1. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-1"></a>**Agency, job, visa approval and the visa itself:** as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job) (FWCMS list, OEP, the BMET permission letter, and BMET's free hand check of the visa at 89/2 Kakrail (02-49357972) or your DEMO: [Step 1, item 6: Find and check the permission letter for your job](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6)) and [Step 3, item 2: Track the approval](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-visa-approval-vdr-item-2) (MyIMMs); BMET's online visa check doesn't cover Malaysia <sup>[26](#o26), [81](#o81), [92](#o92), [1](#o1), [96 (2021)](#o96), [86](#o86)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-2"></a>**Your BMET clearance ([Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight)):** at [portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-5-check-the-visa-yourself-item-3"></a>**After arrival:** IMI's [ePass check](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/checkEpassValid?semakan) shows name, nationality, pass type and expiry when you enter the ePass number (like EP141011C23105313148), passport number and nationality; "Pas Tidak Sah" means not valid <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where does a worker find their ePass number?

**If any check shows a fake visa, pass, job or clearance:** stop paying and keep your passport <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Then:

- complain to BMET and the Ministry's enforcement branch as in [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", with your passport copy, contract, visa, messages with the agency and proof of payment <sup>[1](#o1), [N126](#n126), [100](#o100)</sup>;
- ask the High Commission of Malaysia whether the visa is real (dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918) <sup>[61](#o61), [108](#o108)</sup>;
- report whoever took your money or passport through the online GD ([Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>;
- call 16135 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

<a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight"></a>

### 3.7 Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight

1. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-1"></a>**Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)** at a BMET training institute (list with contacts on [BMET's website](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6)) <sup>[1](#o1), [72](#o72), [73](#o73)</sup>. You'll need your OEP registration and passport or NID number <sup>[19](#o19), [119](#o119)</sup>.

   1. Apply on OEP, then download the enrollment card at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card) with your passport or NID number and the captcha <sup>[19](#o19), [119](#o119)</sup>.
   2. Attend all 3 days and pay against a money receipt (Tk 200 at Feni Technical Training Centre, which says to pay no more) <sup>[1](#o1), [120](#o120)</sup>.
   3. Download your certificate at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate) with your passport or NID number; it is the only real one <sup>[121](#o121)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2"></a>**Check the contract before clearance** (the agency must give you it and a BMET-attested Bangla translation: [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "Before paying") <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>. Get it at least 2 days before you go and check <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>:

   - the job, employer and address, and workplace;
   - length;
   - monthly pay, hours, overtime and leave;
   - airfare;
   - medical care;
   - injury and death compensation;
   - food and housing;
   - return of the body.

   **If there is no contract or translation, or it differs from your permission letter:** don't go to clearance. Then:

   - complain to BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself) <sup>[1](#o1), [100](#o100)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-3"></a>**BMET clearance (the smart card).** After the visa, the agency applies with, for Malaysia, the demand letter, power of attorney and skill certificates, for workers from BMET's database only; BMET's emigration branch clears within 3 working days <sup>[1](#o1), [29](#o29)</sup>. Your costs <sup>[10 (2022)](#o10)</sup>, paid only through the proper channel against a receipt <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>:

   - Tk 5,500 to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the government's welfare board for migrant workers) <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>;
   - a one-time Tk 1,000 insurance premium (5 years' cover, Tk 10 lakh) <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>;
   - a Tk 250 clearance fee <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>;
   - source tax of Tk 800 (unskilled) or Tk 1,200 (skilled) on a group visa <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the Tk 250 clearance fee still apply, given the Ministry reportedly abolished the smart card fee in December 2024 <sup>[N135 (2024)](#n135)</sup>?

   **If you got your visa yourself,** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO with <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>:

   - an application;
   - the visa copy;
   - any contract;
   - an attested copy of your OEP card;
   - a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper;
   - the original pay order for the welfare fee and insurance.

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check; airport welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet stop workers with no or a fake smart card <sup>[117](#o117), [1](#o1), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-4"></a>**The ticket.** The employer pays your air fare both ways, and BMET clears you only once that is ensured; an agency representative must fly with you or meet you in Malaysia <sup>[37 (2024)](#o37), [40 (2022)](#o40), [29](#o29)</sup>.

   **Check the booking is real:** agencies have handed workers fake tickets minutes before the flight <sup>[N136 (2024)](#n136)</sup>. Enter the six-character PNR (booking reference) from your e-ticket on the airline's site:

   - Biman: Manage Booking, with your last name <sup>[N137](#n137)</sup>;
   - Malaysia Airlines: [Manage Booking](https://www.malaysiaairlines.com/ph/en/plan-trip/booking-and-services.html), with your last name <sup>[N138](#n138)</sup>;
   - US-Bangla: [Manage Your Booking](https://usbair.com/manage-your-booking), or use the ticket number <sup>[N139](#n139)</sup>;
   - AirAsia: [Check-in](https://www.airasia.com/check-in/en/gb), with your first name or surname <sup>[N140](#n140)</sup>.

   **Scam:** last-minute demands, such as Tk 50,000, for the ticket, which is the employer's cost <sup>[N136 (2024)](#n136), [37 (2024)](#o37)</sup>. **If asked for ticket money or the ticket is fake:** don't pay. Then:

   - complain to BMET and the Ministry as in [Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself) with the ticket and agency details <sup>[1](#o1), [100](#o100)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-5"></a>**Before you fly,** carry:

   - your passport, contract, visa, BMET clearance, medical report, ticket and receipts <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>;
   - the Bangladesh High Commission's contacts: No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur; 03-2604 0946, 48 or 49; labour wing +60 3 2604 0934 <sup>[125](#o125), [107](#o107)</sup>.

   Leave one set of copies at home and carry 3, and have a bank account in your name and one in a family member's <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>. Keep money and papers in your cabin bag and the checked bag within 20 kg <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>. Never carry a packet whose contents you don't know <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>.

6. <a id="step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-6"></a>**Register the free MDAC (Malaysia Digital Arrival Card)** within 3 days before arrival (the day you submit counts) at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main); workers on a VDR are not exempt, and fake MDAC sites charge for it <sup>[4](#o4), [126](#o126), [N141](#n141), [127 (2024)](#o127), [128](#o128), [129](#o129)</sup>. Fill it in, check it and show it as in [Tourist visa, Step 4: Fill in the Malaysia Digital Arrival Card (MDAC)](#step-4-fill-in-the-malaysia-digital-arrival-card-mdac).

<a id="scams"></a>

### 3.8 Scams

<a id="done-to-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.1 Done to workers

Complain in Bangladesh as under [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job), "If an agency or broker takes your money", and in Malaysia to the labour wing by email <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.

- **Social media posts on Malaysia-approved agencies** have been false <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>; check the FWCMS list instead ([Step 1, item 4: Or go through a private agency](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- **The fee video.** Agencies make workers record a video at their Dhaka office saying they paid only the official fee when they paid far more <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>; keep your own proof of every payment <sup>[N126](#n126)</sup>.
- **Fake passes and permit "agents".** Rings sell fake e-PLKS (electronic work pass) copies for about RM150 and fake police reports for about RM70 <sup>[N109](#n109)</sup>; a Bangladeshi-run ring in Kulim, Kedah, sold fake work permits and fake UNHCR cards for RM80 to 250 <sup>[N142](#n142)</sup>. Only your employer deals with your pass; anyone else offering is breaking the law <sup>[87 (2021)](#o87), [9](#o9)</sup>. Check it yourself ([Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)) <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>.
- **Sabah jobs.** There is no agreement for recruiting Bangladeshis to Sabah; rings have taken passports and cash for Sabah jobs <sup>[N22](#n22)</sup>.
- **No job on arrival.** Employers sign a High Commission commitment to put workers to work at once; if broken, email the labour wing ([After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[25 (2023)](#o25), [130](#o130)</sup>.

<a id="tried-by-workers"></a>

#### 3.8.2 Tried by workers

Forged visas, passes and passports carry jail and caning (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)), including passports bought to resit a failed FOMEMA medical <sup>[55](#o55), [N143](#n143), [N144](#n144)</sup>.

<a id="after-you-arrive"></a>

### 3.9 After you arrive

1. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-1"></a>**Wait for your employer at the airport.** Enter only through official entry points with your VDR and visa; the employer must collect you within 6 hours, take you to the hostel and pay your transport to the workplace <sup>[5](#o5), [131 (2024)](#o131), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>. **If no one comes or there is no job:**

   1. Call the agency representative who must meet the flight <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>.
   2. Then the High Commission's labour wing (+603 2604 0934, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, or +601 2290 3252, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd) <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>.

   Also call 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

   **If there is no job.** Don't take other work, which ends the pass (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>; file at the Labour Office ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) and get free help (see Free help in Malaysia). In one case of 161 Bangladeshis left without work or pay for seven months, the Labour Department told the employer to pay wages owed since arrival and either send the workers home or help them find new employers <sup>[N38 (2024)](#n38)</sup>. In December 2023, 171 Bangladeshis were arrested in Kota Tinggi, Johor, when they went to the police to report their agent <sup>[N37 (2023)](#n37)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a worker whose pass was never issued report to the police without being arrested?

2. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-2"></a>**FOMEMA medical within 30 days.** FOMEMA appoints the clinics for foreign workers' medical checks <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. The employer must take you to a FOMEMA clinic within 30 days of arrival and pay the fee (RM207 for men, RM217 for women (which includes a pregnancy test), plus 8% sales and service tax) <sup>[5](#o5), [36 (2022)](#o36), [132](#o132)</sup>. It includes a chest X-ray, blood tests and a urine drug test for opiates, methamphetamine and cannabis; results usually come within 7 working days <sup>[133](#o133), [134](#o134)</sup>. Check yours on IMI's [FOMEMA Application Status](https://eservices.imi.gov.my/myimms/FomemaStatus?lang=en) with your passport number and BANGLADESH <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>; the employer checks it on the FOMEMA portal, and neither of you sees the report itself <sup>[136](#o136), [N80](#n80)</sup>.

   **If the employer hasn't taken you within 30 days:** without a FOMEMA fit result there is no work pass (PLKS) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Then:

   - complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000) <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106), [97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>;
   - tell the labour wing ([After you arrive, item 1: Wait for your employer at the airport](#after-you-arrive-item-1)) <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>;
   - call 16135 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

   **If you are found unfit:** no PLKS. You are sent home with a Check-Out Memo (the employer's application for a foreign worker to leave), and the employer pays the fare <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9), [37 (2024)](#o37)</sup>. Only the employer or agency can appeal, for a fee, on portal.fomema.my within 2 weeks; the case closes if extra test results don't arrive within 4 weeks <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>. Confirmed tuberculosis, malaria, pregnancy, syphilis, HIV and hepatitis B can't be appealed <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>. Ask the employer in writing to appeal, and tell the labour wing <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>. Using a false passport to sit FOMEMA again carries 2 to 10 years' jail (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>.

3. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-3"></a>**PLKS sticker and i-Kad.** The sticker is printed once IMI approves your PLKS and the employer pays (CETAK on MyIMMs) <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>. Every PLKS holder gets an i-Kad (foreign worker identity card), free and without applying; the employer collects it from Immigration 7 days after IMI's email <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>. Carry it always and show it when asked <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>. If you don't get it, complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000), then the labour wing <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106), [97 (2017)](#o97), [107](#o107)</sup>.

4. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-4"></a>**Keep your own passport.** Your employer may not keep your passport; hand it over only for the medical, PLKS, i-Kad and renewal, and get it back <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>. Holding someone else's passport without lawful authority carries a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both <sup>[44 (1997)](#o44), [138](#o138)</sup>. **If your employer keeps it:**

   1. Ask for it back in writing and keep a copy <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>.
   2. Complain to the Labour Department (WFW app, 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my) <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106), [97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>.
   3. Tell the labour wing <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>.

   For unpaid wages, see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid).

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does a report to the Malaysian police or IMI get a passport back?
   > - Where does a worker make that report?

5. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-5"></a>**Wages into your own bank account** (the rules: Your rights at work, Pay) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. The employer is expected to open it <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. At Maybank, for example, open it at a branch (the app needs a Malaysian identity card) with your passport and at least RM250, and perhaps your work permit, visa and an employer's letter <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.

6. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-6"></a>**SIM card.** Register every prepaid SIM yourself, in person, at an operator's service centre or authorised dealer with your original passport and work-permit document <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. The limit is 2 prepaid SIMs per operator; activation is confirmed within 12 hours <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. Don't use a SIM that was active before you registered it <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>. Don't give documents or codes to anyone offering to "help"; each operator checks free which numbers are in your name <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.

7. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-7"></a>**Social security, insurance and the retirement fund.** The employer must register you with PERKESO (SOCSO, Malaysia's social security organisation) for the Employment Injury Scheme (accidents, work diseases, death at work; employer pays 1.25% of wages) and the Invalidity Scheme (critical illness or death in Malaysia before 60 from any cause; employer 0.5%, you 0.5%) <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. You get a 12-digit social security number; SOCSO: 1-300-22-8000 <sup>[141 (2019)](#o141), [142 (2022)](#o142)</sup>. Not registering or paying is an offence for the employer <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>.

   SPIKPA, the compulsory hospital and surgery insurance, costs RM120 a year; the employer buys it with the PLKS application and may deduct it from wages with Labour Department permission, and cover starts once you are registered and it is paid <sup>[143](#o143), [N145](#n145), [144](#o144)</sup>. At a government hospital show your valid passport; you pay no deposit and only the part of a bill above the cover limit <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>. An unpaid government hospital bill blocks your PLKS renewal <sup>[144](#o144)</sup>.

   EPF (Employees Provident Fund, the retirement fund; 2% from you and 2% from the employer, Your rights at work) registers you automatically; visit any EPF office, no appointment, to update your details and give your thumbprint <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. When you leave for good, withdraw your savings before you fly ([Leaving at the end of the job, item 4: Withdraw your EPF before you fly](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4)) <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

8. <a id="after-you-arrive-item-8"></a>**If you came without BMET clearance,** join the Wage Earners' Welfare Board for its insurance and help: apply at [portal.wewb.gov.bd](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/) with your passport and work permit; the labour wing processes it, for the ringgit equivalent of Tk 6,500, life insurance included <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. Workers with BMET clearance are already covered (check at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>.

<a id="your-rights-at-work"></a>

### 3.10 Your rights at work

Your terms may be no worse than the Employment Act 1955 (the main labour law in Peninsular Malaysia): a worse term is void and replaced by the Act, and no contract may stop you joining a registered trade union <sup>[36 (2022)](#o36), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. The employer or its agent must explain the terms when you are selected, the contract you sign must carry the same terms, and you are entitled to your own copy from the employer named on your work permit <sup>[36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>. Under the Bangladesh-Malaysia agreement the contract is in Malay, Bangla and English, and the English counts <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. These rights are Peninsular Malaysia's and Labuan's; Sabah and Sarawak have their own Labour Ordinances <sup>[42](#o42)</sup>. For any broken right, use the routes under If you aren't paid.

<a id="contract"></a>

#### 3.10.1 Contract

<a id="pay-and-deductions"></a>

#### 3.10.2 Pay and deductions

**Pay**
Wages are paid monthly by the 7th of the next month into your own bank account ([After you arrive, item 5: Wages into your own bank account](#after-you-arrive-item-5)); overtime, rest-day and holiday pay by the end of the next wage period <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Your basic wage must meet the minimum wage and equal a local worker's for the same job <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>. The employer may not dictate where, how or with whom you spend it <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. The minimum is RM1,700 a month or RM8.72 an hour (by the day: RM65.38 on a 6-day week, RM78.46 on 5 days, RM98.08 on 4) <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11)</sup>, for every employer, including for piece, task, trip or commission pay <sup>[11 (2024)](#o11)</sup>. It is to rise to RM2,000 a month from June 2027, with micro, small and medium businesses selling under RM50 million a year exempt <sup>[146](#o146)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Minimum Wages Order setting RM2,000 been made?

**Deductions**
Only what the Employment Act allows <sup>[147](#o147), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Rent, food or payments to anyone else need your written request and Labour Department approval, given for rent and food only if they benefit you <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. All deductions together may not exceed half a month's wages <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. In Sarawak nothing may be deducted for recruitment or repatriation, and foreign workers get local workers' rate <sup>[43](#o43)</sup>.

<a id="hours-rest-and-leave"></a>

#### 3.10.3 Hours, rest and leave

**Hours and overtime**
At most 8 hours a day and 45 a week, no more than 5 hours in a row without a 30-minute break, and never over 12 hours a day except in emergencies <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33), [148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>. Overtime is only at the employer's request with your agreement, paid at least 1.5 times the hourly rate (for monthly pay: the monthly wage divided by 26, then by your normal daily hours) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

**Rest days and holidays**
One rest day a week, on which you can't be made to work except in continuous shift work or emergencies <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Work on it within normal hours pays, on monthly pay, half a day's pay for up to half the normal hours and a full day's for more, on top of the monthly wage, and on daily pay one or two days' wages; beyond normal hours, at least 2 times the hourly rate <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. 11 paid public holidays a year; working one earns 2 extra days' wages, and overtime that day at least 3 times the hourly rate <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Absence without consent on the working day before or after a holiday loses that holiday's pay unless you have a reasonable excuse <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

**Leave**
Paid annual leave is 8, 12 or 16 days a year (under 2, 2 to 5, over 5 years' service) <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>; paid sick leave 14, 18 or 22 days on the same bands, plus up to 60 days if a doctor certifies hospital care <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33), [149 (2024)](#o149)</sup>; it needs a doctor's certificate and notice to the employer within 48 hours, or it counts as absence <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Maternity leave is 98 days <sup>[147](#o147)</sup>. A married man gets 7 days' paid paternity leave per birth, up to 5 births, after 12 months' service, if he tells the employer at least 30 days before the expected birth <sup>[148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>.

<a id="housing-food-and-health-care"></a>

#### 3.10.4 Housing, food and health care

Employer housing, under Act 446 (the Employees' Minimum Standards of Housing, Accommodations and Amenities Act 1990), needs a Labour Department Certificate for Accommodation, men and women live separately, and the person in charge must take ill workers to a clinic or hospital <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [36 (2022)](#o36), [150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. Each worker gets his own bed with a 4-inch mattress, pillow, blanket and lockable cupboard <sup>[N69 (2020)](#n69)</sup>, at least 3.6 square metres in a bedroom (3 in a dormitory), and no more than 6 share a bathroom and toilet (15 in a dormitory) <sup>[N69 (2020)](#n69)</sup>. On estates and other workplaces outside city, municipal and Federal Territory areas, employer housing must have free piped water and electricity, and no rent may be charged <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. Elsewhere rent can be taken from wages only within limits set by regulations, at most RM100 a month per worker <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150), [N69 (2020)](#n69)</sup>. Many employers still use hostels that break Act 446, and some leave workers to buy their own beds and kitchen equipment <sup>[N146](#n146)</sup>; the Labour Department can inspect at any time without notice <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. If the job ends with under 4 weeks' notice, you may stay up to 4 weeks <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the RM100 monthly rent limit changed since 2020?

<a id="your-passport"></a>

#### 3.10.5 Your passport

The employer may not keep it ([After you arrive, item 4: Keep your own passport](#after-you-arrive-item-4)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [44 (1997)](#o44)</sup>; if it is kept, you can also complain to SUHAKAM (the Human Rights Commission of Malaysia; see Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.

<a id="end-of-service-pay"></a>

#### 3.10.6 End-of-service pay

EPF (the retirement fund, [After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7)): the employer pays 2% of your wage and deducts 2% from you, by the 15th of the next month, and you withdraw it all when you leave for good ([Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)) <sup>[34](#o34), [145](#o145)</sup>. PERKESO (social security, [After you arrive, item 7: Social security, insurance and the retirement fund](#after-you-arrive-item-7)) also pays invalidity and survivors' pensions (see [If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job](#if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job)) <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>. Bangladesh's Probash pension is open to Bangladeshis aged 18 to 50 working abroad <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>:

- a bank account;
- a mobile number;
- an email address;
- a foreign-currency card.

You can name a nominee, who gets the money if you die <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

1. Register yourself, in your own name, at [upension.gov.bd](https://www.upension.gov.bd/) under Probash, with no third person; your ID, rate and payment dates come by SMS and email, and any Sonali Bank branch is the front office <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.
2. Pay Tk 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 7,500 or 10,000 a month, 3, 9 or 12 months at a time, by card to Sonali Bank; 2.5% is added from abroad <sup>[153](#o153), [152](#o152)</sup>.

What you get back: after 10 years of unbroken payments, a monthly pension into your bank from age 60 without applying (Tk 1,000 a month for 20 years gives about Tk 4,927 a month); if you die, all you paid plus profit goes to your nominee <sup>[152](#o152), [153](#o153)</sup>. On returning home you can switch to the Progoti or Surokkha scheme <sup>[152](#o152)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What details does the Probash pension registration form ask for?
> - Does registering for the Probash pension cost anything?
> - How can a worker pay the Probash pension without a foreign-currency card?
> - What can a worker do if Probash pension registration or payment fails?

<a id="your-residence-permit"></a>

### 3.11 Your residence permit

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay-and-renewing"></a>

#### 3.11.1 How long you can stay and renewing

**12 months at a time, up to 10 years**
The PLKS lasts 12 months <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. Only the employer renews it, from 3 months before expiry, and pays any late penalty <sup>[9](#o9), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Renewal needs a passport valid over 12 months, a FOMEMA medical every year, and SPIKPA hospital insurance (except in plantations) <sup>[5](#o5), [134](#o134), [24](#o24)</sup>. A new 48-page, 5-year e-passport from the High Commission in Kuala Lumpur costs a worker RM172 (RM140 fee plus a RM32 ESKL charge for the appointment, enrolment and delivery) <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>; Bangladesh missions charge workers USD 50 for 10 years (express USD 45 for 5 years, USD 75 for 10) <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>. A renewal filed after expiry counts as overstay <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>. The limit is 10 years in total, or 3 for workers registered under the 6P legalisation programme <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>.

**If the employer doesn't renew,** you risk arrest <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Check your pass on Immigration's ePass check ([Step 5: Check the visa yourself](#step-5-check-the-visa-yourself)) <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>; if it is near expiry and unrenewed, email the labour wing and contact Tenaganita ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[130](#o130), [N147](#n147)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Labour Department take a complaint about a pass that isn't renewed?
> - Are Bangladeshi workers' renewals still done only at Immigration Putrajaya <sup>[87 (2021)](#o87)</sup> now that work-pass applications go through FWCMS <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>?

<a id="going-home-on-leave-and-coming-back"></a>

#### 3.11.2 Going home on leave and coming back

The yearly PLKS renewal includes a multiple-entry visa for Bangladeshi workers, with no separate application <sup>[N148](#n148)</sup>. Agree your return date with the employer; missing it counts as running away <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker check the multiple-entry visa was issued?

<a id="bringing-your-family"></a>

#### 3.11.3 Bringing your family

PLKS workers can't bring family <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9)</sup>. For professionals' routes, see [Family passes](#family-passes); for registering a birth <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>, see [Having a child in Malaysia](#having-a-child-in-malaysia).

PLKS workers can't bring family or marry in Malaysia ([Spouse of a Malaysian citizen](#spouse-of-a-malaysian-citizen)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>, and can't apply for permanent residence; PLKS years don't count toward it <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

**No family, marriage or permanent residence**
PLKS holders can't bring family, may not marry a Malaysian or another foreigner while on the pass, can't apply for permanent residence, and must go home when the pass ends <sup>[5](#o5), [9](#o9), [N149 (2021)](#n149), [N150 (2021)](#n150)</sup>.

**Pregnancy and the work pass**
The yearly FOMEMA medical includes a pregnancy test for women <sup>[132](#o132), [134](#o134)</sup>. Confirmed pregnancy means unfit, with no appeal, and the worker is sent home with a Check-Out Memo <sup>[137](#o137), [5](#o5)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What happens to a worker who becomes pregnant between yearly medicals?

A pregnant worker can't be dismissed except for wilful breach of contract, misconduct or closure of the business <sup>[149 (2024)](#o149), [148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>, but confirmed pregnancy at the yearly FOMEMA medical still ends the work pass <sup>[137](#o137), [5](#o5)</sup>.

<a id="what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported"></a>

### 3.12 What can get you jailed or deported

<a id="no-valid-pass-or-passport"></a>

#### 3.12.1 No valid pass or passport

Entering illegally, or failing to show a valid passport and pass when asked: fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, and up to 6 strokes of the cane, or a compound (out-of-court fine) up to RM3,000 <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Carry your i-Kad at all times <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>. Overstaying an expired or cancelled pass: fine of at least RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, or a compound up to RM3,000 <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [46](#o46), [55](#o55)</sup>. Breaking a pass condition, such as working for another employer, ends the pass at once <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [5](#o5)</sup>; PLKS holders may not work as frontliners (customer-facing staff) <sup>[5](#o5)</sup>.

<a id="after-arrest"></a>

#### 3.12.2 After arrest

Someone whose pass has expired or been cancelled is removed whether or not prosecuted, held as long as removal takes, with no appeal <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. On entering an Immigration depot your biometrics are taken and you are blacklisted <sup>[N87 (2022)](#n87)</sup>. Depots have held more people than their capacity, and former detainees describe medical care denied or delayed <sup>[N106](#n106)</sup>. Family can call the High Commission's consular line, +603 2604 0949, or email the labour wing; its officials visit camps <sup>[125](#o125), [130](#o130), [N151](#n151)</sup>. For visiting a detainee and the flight home, see [If you are detained](#if-you-are-detained).

> **Need to know**
> - Does Immigration's permission letter to visit a detainee cost anything?
> - How long does Immigration take to issue the permission letter to visit a detainee?

<a id="false-papers"></a>

#### 3.12.3 False papers

Making, forging or using a forged visa or pass: fine of at least RM30,000, 5 to 10 years' jail, up to 6 strokes <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. A false passport: RM15,000 to RM100,000, 2 to 10 years, up to 6 strokes <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Letting someone else hold your passport, intending or knowing it may be used by another person, is also an offence <sup>[44 (1997)](#o44)</sup>.

<a id="drugs"></a>

#### 3.12.4 Drugs

Trafficking means death or life imprisonment, plus at least 12 strokes if not sentenced to death <sup>[N152](#n152), [158 (2023)](#o158), [159](#o159)</sup>.

<a id="banned-goods"></a>

#### 3.12.5 Banned goods

Obscene pictures or films on any media, daggers, flick knives and cloth printed with Quran verses may not be brought in; declare anything dutiable and expect your bags to be opened <sup>[159](#o159)</sup>.

<a id="if-something-goes-wrong"></a>

### 3.13 If something goes wrong

<a id="if-you-arent-paid"></a>

#### 3.13.1 If you aren't paid

Photograph your contract, payslips, attendance records, passport and pass, and note the employer's registered name and address, from SSM e-Info if you don't have them ([Step 1, item 7: Check the employer exists](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7)) <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [N82 (2021)](#n82), [49](#o49)</sup>. Without a written contract you are still presumed an employee if the other person controls your work or hours, provides tools, or pays you regularly as most of your income <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. If you work for a subcontractor, the main contractor is also liable for up to 3 months' unpaid wages if you complain within 90 days of the due date <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. If the employer seems about to run off to avoid paying, complain to a Magistrate, who can require a bond or order arrest <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Use the routes below in order; several at once is fine.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker reach a Magistrate to complain about an employer about to run off?

**1. Working for Workers (WFW) app**, the Ministry of Human Resources' complaint channel <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[N153 (2021)](#n153)</sup>:

- a smartphone;
- your name, mobile, passport number, gender and nationality;
- the company's name, phone and workplace address (state, district and postcode).

1. Install "WFW" from Google Play or the App Store (official page: [workforworkers.com.my](https://www.workforworkers.com.my/sapn-portal/index)) and register <sup>[106](#o106), [160](#o160), [N153 (2021)](#n153)</sup>.
2. Choose the complaint type (unpaid, late or below-minimum wages, no overtime pay, unlawful deductions, housing) and describe what happened <sup>[N153 (2021)](#n153), [105](#o105)</sup>.

What you get back: the complaint goes to the labour authorities, your identity is kept confidential, and you track it in the app; no Labour Office visit is needed <sup>[106](#o106), [160](#o160)</sup>. At launch the ministry said complaints would be dealt with within 3 to 7 days <sup>[N153 (2021)](#n153)</sup>.

**In practice:** claims without payslips are hard to prove; complainers have been warned off, falsely reported absent or had visas cancelled (If your employer reports you absent (lari)) <sup>[45](#o45), [N96 (2024)](#n96), [N61](#n61)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the WFW app cost anything?
> - Does the WFW app work in Bangla?
> - What can a worker do if a WFW complaint gets no response?

**2. The High Commission's labour wing**, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>. You'll need: the details on the route 5 checklist ([Where to complain, in Malaysia and from Bangladesh](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), route 5: Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Bangladesh) <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

1. Email counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com or fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com, or call +601 2290 3252; a group can send one email <sup>[130](#o130), [107](#o107), [N154](#n154)</sup>.
2. If it doesn't act, apply to the Ministry ([Where to complain, in Malaysia and from Bangladesh](#where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh), route 5: Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Bangladesh), which tells it to <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

What happens: it calls the employer in, visits the workplace and attends Labour Department hearings, and takes ignored cases to the Ministry of Human Resources <sup>[N154](#n154), [N155 (2023)](#n155)</sup>.

**In practice:** it recovered compensation on 2,857 claims in 2025 <sup>[N156](#n156)</sup>; other workers say complaints to it and the Labour Department brought nothing until a migrant rights group stepped in (see Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[N157](#n157), [N70](#n70)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does the labour wing take to reply?
> - Does the labour wing charge anything?

**3. Labour Court claim**, the Labour Department's (JTK's) free wage tribunal, with no upper limit <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. The employer may not stop you going to it <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [N82 (2021)](#n82), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>:

- your passport (or a copy);
- the employer's name and address;
- payslips;
- contract or appointment letter;
- any termination letter;
- arrival date;
- witnesses' names.

1. For free advice call 03-8000 8000. Then give a sworn statement to an officer at the nearest Labour Office (Pejabat Tenaga Kerja), or complain by letter or to jtksm@mohr.gov.my <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [33 (2023)](#o33), [N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>. JTK's complaint pages and hotline are not in Bangla <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>.
2. The officer sets a mention date (a first short hearing), summons the employer and tells you when and where; bring your witnesses <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.
3. Attend every date; an absent employer can lose by default <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Workers with the same claim can file together through representatives <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. No lawyers, but a trade union can help <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>.

What you get back: a written order to pay, with up to 8% yearly interest, enforced through the courts (for example by seizing property) if unpaid <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33), [97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>. Either side can appeal to the High Court within 14 days <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>.

Time: about a month if settled, about 3 months if heard <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>. Challenge a misconduct dismissal within 60 days of being told of it <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

If your pass ends mid-case: take a Labour Office letter showing the pending case to Immigration's Foreign Workers Division for a Special Pass (one month, extendable; usually granted with at least two extensions; decided within 3 working days; RM100). It doesn't let you work <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82), [88](#o88), [138](#o138), [161](#o161)</sup>.

**In practice:** about half of claims settle at the mention and about 85% of those heard are decided for the worker, but 38% are withdrawn or dismissed for non-attendance, usually because the pass expired <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>; employers often stall until the pass runs out, hence the Special Pass above <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>. An order is not money: Kawaguchi's workers were awarded over RM3 million in wages but got only RM251,000 after the company held back pay for months and closed <sup>[N71](#n71)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where is the official list of Labour Office addresses?
> - Can a worker apply for the Special Pass himself?
> - Does each Special Pass extension cost RM100?

**In Sabah and Sarawak** complain to the state's own Labour Department, under its Labour Ordinance <sup>[162](#o162), [163](#o163)</sup>.

- **Sarawak.** Complain to the labour office nearest the workplace, any of these ways <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>:
  - in writing or on its Borang Aduan Pekerja form, by post, fax or email;
  - in person;
  - by phone;
  - on SISPAA (the Malaysian government's online complaints system);
  - on the WFW app.

  Give your and the employer's name, address and phone, the issues, and your contract and payslips <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>. A Labour Court claim is made in person by sworn statement, with your passport, the employer's name and address, contract, pay details and the amount claimed <sup>[163](#o163)</sup>. Headquarters: Tingkat 13, Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Jalan Simpang Tiga, 93532 Kuching; 082-242261; jtknsarawak@mohr.gov.my <sup>[164](#o164)</sup>.
- **Sabah.** Complain to JTK Sabah, jtksabah.gov.my <sup>[162](#o162)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - What are JTK Sabah's office addresses and phone numbers?
  > - Does JTK Sabah take WFW complaints?

<a id="if-you-are-abused-denied-food-or-medical-care-or-your-passport-is-taken"></a>

#### 3.13.2 If you are abused, denied food or medical care, or your passport is taken

**Forced labour**
An employer who threatens, deceives or forces you to work and stops you leaving can be fined up to RM100,000, jailed up to 2 years, or both <sup>[148 (2022)](#o148)</sup>. Call the 24-hour emergency line of Tenaganita (a Malaysian NGO for migrant workers), the labour wing or SUHAKAM ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[N158](#n158), [130](#o130), [151](#o151)</sup>.

**Sexual harassment**
Report it to the Labour Department's Director General, who can direct the employer to inquire <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. A woman worker or a close relative in Bangladesh can also apply free to the Ministry, aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, with passport, smart card and residence-permit copies, the agency's details and the accused person's contacts; it acts within 7 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker file a sexual harassment complaint with the Labour Department's Director General?

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill"></a>

#### 3.13.3 If you are injured or fall ill

<a id="if-your-employer-reports-you-absent-lari"></a>

#### 3.13.4 If your employer reports you absent (lari)

**What counts**
Leaving the workplace without telling the employer, intending to escape, or not returning on the agreed date after a trip home <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>.

**What follows**
The employer reports you to Immigration with its ID, your passport copy, the runaway form and a police report, and tells the Labour Department within 14 days <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [148 (2022)](#o148), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. You are blacklisted and its security bond is forfeited <sup>[5](#o5), [24](#o24)</sup>. Immigration normally cancels a quitting worker's pass at once, leaving you without a valid pass <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>. False reports are filed near the contract's end to avoid the fare home, or after a complaint <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.

**What you can do**
1. Keep photos of your pass, payslips and attendance records, and details of any WFW or Labour Office complaint <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97), [106](#o106)</sup>.
2. If you leave over abuse or unpaid wages, file at the Labour Office and email the labour wing <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131), [130](#o130)</sup>; the pending-case letter usually gets you a Special Pass ([If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>.
3. If you are arrested, family can call Tenaganita's 24-hour line and email the labour wing <sup>[N158](#n158), [130](#o130)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker find out about an absence report or blacklisting, given Immigration's online check is for citizens only <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>?
> - How does a worker clear a false absence report?

<a id="where-to-complain-in-malaysia-and-from-bangladesh"></a>

#### 3.13.5 Where to complain, in Malaysia and from Bangladesh

**Free help in Malaysia**
Unrepresented workers in the 2024 Pengerang mediation settled for about half their RM2.1 million claim, while 74 workers helped by Tenaganita won a Labour Office order for RM1.54 million <sup>[N159 (2024)](#n159), [N160](#n160)</sup>. The Welfare Board has no safe house in Malaysia <sup>[166](#o166)</sup>.

- Tenaganita (an NGO that takes migrant workers' cases, and helps gather the documents a Labour Department claim needs): 12 Jalan 6/11, 46000 Petaling Jaya, Selangor, +603 7770 3671 or 3691, Monday to Friday 9:30 to 6:00, general@tenaganita.net; 24-hour emergency lines for abuse, rescue, arrest or detention +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 <sup>[N147](#n147), [N158](#n158), [N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>.
- Bar Council Legal Aid Centres give non-citizens free legal advice in every Peninsular state, walk-in or by appointment; bring your documents <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>. Kuala Lumpur: Unit 3.03, Level 3, Wisma Badan Peguam Malaysia, 2 Leboh Pasar Besar, 50050 Kuala Lumpur, +603 2691 1121 or 2692 1122, lac.kl@malaysianbar.org.my <sup>[N161](#n161)</sup>. They advise and help negotiate; representation is rare, criminal only, and may cost money <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82), [N161](#n161)</sup>.
- SUHAKAM investigates complaints from anyone, including non-citizens, about exploitation or unpaid wages, and recommends action to the authorities <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>. Email aduan@suhakam.org.my, call +603-2612 5635 or 5636, write to 17th Floor, Menara Aras Raya, Jalan Raja Laut, Kuala Lumpur, or use "New Complaint" on suhakam.org.my and keep the Ticket ID <sup>[151](#o151)</sup>.
- MTUC (the Malaysian Trades Union Congress) has run a migrant worker centre at 10-5 Jalan USJ 9/5T, Subang Business Centre, 47620 Subang Jaya, Selangor, +603 8024 2953 or +6017 680 9384 <sup>[N162 (2018)](#n162)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - Does the MTUC migrant worker centre still take cases?

**5. Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Bangladesh** (you or your family). You'll need <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>:

- what happened;
- proof you are a migrant worker (passport, work permit, BMET smart card, Malaysian ID number);
- the employer's name and address;
- what you are owed.

Send it free to the Welfare-1 branch: saswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030251 <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

What you get back: within 7 working days the Ministry asks the labour wing or the Wage Earners' Welfare Board to act, and tells you by letter <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>. Advice any time: 16135 in Bangladesh (free) or +880 9610102030 from abroad <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

**Injured at work:** claim through PERKESO (see [If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job](#if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job)) <sup>[35](#o35)</sup>; to claim from the employer, file at the Labour Office with payslips, contract and attendance record, and copy the High Commission <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>.

<a id="changing-employer-or-leaving"></a>

### 3.14 Changing employer or leaving

<a id="if-you-want-to-change-employer"></a>

#### 3.14.1 If you want to change employer

**You can't apply yourself**
PLKS holders may not change employer or sector on their own, and the agreement contract bars it <sup>[5](#o5), [40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Only the employer or its appointed company representative deals with Immigration; using an agent is an offence <sup>[87 (2021)](#o87)</sup>.

**When it is allowed**
Only if all of these hold:

- the employer is bankrupt, closed, has suffered a disaster such as a fire or economic crisis and can't keep its workers, has been restructured or renamed, or has died <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>;
- the new employer is in the same sector and allowed foreign workers <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>;
- your PLKS has 3 months left <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.

Not open in your 10th year or later, to workers legalised through 6P, RTK (Labour Recalibration) or other programmes, or to domestic helpers <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are cross-sector moves now possible, given Immigration says they need Home Ministry (KDN) permission <sup>[87 (2021)](#o87)</sup> but they have been reported as allowed <sup>[N163](#n163)</sup>?

**How the employers do it**
1. Both employers register on ePPAx (eppax.gov.my, the Labour Department's employer system); the old employer applies naming the new one, which uploads its documents within 7 days <sup>[167 (2023)](#o167), [168 (2023)](#o168)</sup>.
2. The Labour Department investigates and issues a support letter <sup>[167 (2023)](#o167), [41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.
3. The old employer (or heir) submits the free Ministry of Human Resources form and documents at the foreign workers division, Level 5, Setia Perkasa 3, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 6501, Monday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4.30 pm, queue numbers 8.30 am to noon only <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does a change of employer take?

<a id="if-you-resign-or-are-dismissed"></a>

#### 3.14.2 If you resign or are dismissed

Under the agreement contract, either side gives 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. Not paying wages counts as the employer breaking the contract; either side may end it without notice for a wilful breach by the other, and you may leave at once if you or your dependants face immediate violence or disease you didn't agree to risk <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Being absent more than 2 working days in a row without leave or a reasonable excuse told to the employer counts as you breaking it <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Dismissal for misconduct needs an inquiry first; suspension during it lasts at most 2 weeks on at least half pay <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. In a redundancy, foreign workers in a similar job go before any local worker <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. After 12 months, if the employer ends the job (except for misconduct), you get at least 10 days' wages per year of service under 2 years, 15 days for 2 to 5 years, 20 days for 5 or more, pro rata <sup>[N164](#n164), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

<a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job"></a>

#### 3.14.3 Leaving at the end of the job

1. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-1"></a>**Check-Out Memo** (Memo Periksa Keluar, Immigration's approval to leave for home). The employer applies to Immigration with the form, your original passport and a ticket; you leave on the date and through the exit point on the memo, and the employer is responsible for you until then <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. The Home Minister announced in May 2025 that employers must also be present at the exit point <sup>[N84](#n84)</sup>.
2. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-2"></a>**Ticket home.**
   - The employer pays when the contract ends, when it ends the contract early (unless you breached it), when you leave because it broke the contract, or after serious work injury or illness <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>.
   - You pay if you resign or are dismissed for misconduct <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>.
3. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-3"></a>**What you are owed:**
   - wages by the last day of the job, or within 3 days if you end it without notice <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>;
   - all outstanding wages if sent home early, untaken annual leave (unless dismissed for misconduct), and termination benefits if the employer ended the job after 12 months ([Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40), [33 (2023)](#o33), [N164](#n164)</sup>.
4. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-4"></a>**Withdraw your EPF before you fly.** You'll need <sup>[145](#o145), [169](#o169)</sup>:
   - Form KWSP 9K (AHL) and checklist;
   - a copy of your valid passport (or a High Commission travel permit);
   - the Check-Out Memo;
   - a bank document for an active account at an EPF panel bank in the passport details EPF holds.

   1. Apply as soon as you have the memo <sup>[169](#o169)</sup>.
   2. Apply in person at the self-service terminal in an EPF office, with copies certified by an EPF officer, the High Commission or your employer; helpline +603-8922 6000 <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - Where is EPF's official list of offices?
      > - Does EPF withdrawal cost anything?
      > - How long does EPF take to pay out?

   What you get back: all your savings in ringgit into that account, so keep it open, or a banker's cheque if payment fails <sup>[145](#o145), [169](#o169)</sup>.

   Already left? Use EPF's overseas application: post the forms, certified by a Malaysian high commission or notary, to EPF, Jabatan Pengurusan Transaksi, Menara KWSP, No.1 Persiaran Kwasa Utama, Kwasa Damansara, Seksyen U4, 40150 Shah Alam, Selangor; the money is sent by bank transfer <sup>[145](#o145)</sup>.

   The EPF account then closes; returning workers register again <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.
5. <a id="leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job-item-5"></a>**To leave** you need a passport valid 6 months and a valid pass <sup>[170](#o170)</sup>.

<a id="work-visa-domestic-worker"></a>

## 4 Work visa: domestic worker

This visa is not open to Bangladeshis. Immigration's approved source countries for domestic helpers are Indonesia, Thailand, Cambodia, the Philippines, Sri Lanka, India, Vietnam, Laos and Nepal <sup>[17](#o17), [9](#o9)</sup>.

<a id="student-visa-student-pass"></a>

## 5 Student visa (Student Pass)

A Student Pass lets you stay in Malaysia to study full time at an approved institution; it is not a work visa <sup>[53](#o53), [52](#o52)</sup>. You apply through EMGS (Education Malaysia Global Services, a company under Malaysia's Ministry of Higher Education, KPT), for public and private institutions alike <sup>[171](#o171), [52](#o52), [172](#o172)</sup>; the Immigration Department of Malaysia (IMI) gives final approval <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course"></a>

### 5.1 Step 1: Check the institution and the course

Check before you pay anyone: agents earn commission from institutions and push the colleges paying most <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-1"></a>**Check the institution may take foreign students.** A private institution needs KPT approval, an accredited programme and the Home Ministry's (KDN's) permission to take international students; a public university needs no separate check <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. KPT's Department of Higher Education (JPT) lists private institutions with that permission, updated monthly <sup>[173](#o173), [174](#o174)</sup>.
   1. On [jpt.mohe.gov.my, Senarai Daftar dan Statistik IPT Swasta](https://jpt.mohe.gov.my/portal/index.php/ms/ipt/ipt-swasta/25-senarai-daftar-dan-statistik-ipt-swasta), click the download icon beside "Senarai Daftar IPTS Kelulusan KDN" <sup>[173](#o173)</sup>.
   2. Open "Tahun 2026", then the "AKTIF" (active) file with the highest number (in November 2026, file 7, the list up to 31 July 2026, updated 20 August 2026) <sup>[175](#o175), [176](#o176), [177](#o177)</sup>.
   3. Search it for the institution's name; the campus address must match the offer letter <sup>[177](#o177)</sup>.

   If the institution is missing, is in the "TIDAK AKTIF" (inactive) file of the same number, or is on the page's list of cancelled approvals ("Senarai IPTS Yang Telah Diluluskan Tindakan Pembatalan Kelulusan Penubuhan"), it can't get you a Student Pass, since IMI needs its KDN approval letter, in force, with every new application <sup>[176](#o176), [178](#o178), [173](#o173), [52](#o52)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-2"></a>**Check the course is accredited** on the [Malaysian Qualifications Register (MQR)](https://www2.mqa.gov.my/mqr/) of MQA (the Malaysian Qualifications Agency): type the course in "Name of Qualification", choose the level and press Search <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>. Only accredited programmes appear, and only for the campus named; a course run anywhere else is not accredited <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>. "No record" can mean provisional accreditation only, never accredited, or failed <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-3"></a>**A language or training centre** needs approval from the Ministry of Education (KPM) and the Ministry of Human Resources (KESUMA), plus KDN's permission to take international students <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>, and its students can't work part time or bring family <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. To check one, contact KPM's Private Education Division: 03-8884 9536, bps@moe.gov.my, Aras 3, Blok E2, Kompleks E, 62604 Putrajaya; it acknowledges enquiries within 3 working days <sup>[180](#o180)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is there a public list of KPM- or KESUMA-approved centres allowed to take international students?
   > - Where can you check a KESUMA-approved centre?

4. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-4"></a>**Check you qualify:** 12 years of schooling and a recognised exam <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>, and an English certificate, without which the application may be delayed and the pass won't cover the whole course <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>. The certificates accepted are:
   - MUET (the Malaysian University English Test) <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - IELTS <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - TOEFL iBT or Essentials <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - PTE Academic <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - Cambridge English <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - Linguaskill <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - OET <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - ELS CIEP <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-1-check-the-institution-and-the-course-item-5"></a>**Check the offer letter is real.** Agents have given Bangladeshi students fake ones <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. The institution's international office can confirm by email or phone that it is genuine and the course is running <sup>[N101](#n101)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is there an official EMGS or KPT channel for checking an offer letter?
   > - Where can you find the international office's genuine contacts?

<a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh"></a>

### 5.2 Step 2: Apply from Bangladesh

Apply from outside Malaysia <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. You or the institution can create the application <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>, but only one you create and pay for yourself on the Education Malaysia website can be refunded to you <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>. You can track it without an agent ([Step 4: Check your visa is real](#step-4-check-your-visa-is-real)) <sup>[183](#o183), [N101](#n101)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-1"></a>**Get and accept an offer letter;** the form can't be submitted without it <sup>[53](#o53), [181](#o181)</sup>.
2. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-2"></a>**You'll need:**
   - a 35 x 45 mm photo on white <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - your passport's data, visa and observation pages <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>, the passport valid at least 18 months (for a pass covering the whole course, EMGS recommends 12 months beyond the course) <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - the offer letter, all pages, as one PDF <sup>[181](#o181), [184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>;
   - EMGS's Health Declaration Form <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - your certificates and transcripts <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>;
   - your English certificate <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.

   Keep your passport: agents have held students' passports to demand more money <sup>[N101](#n101), [N165](#n165)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-3"></a>**Open an account and apply** on [visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my](https://visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my/) or the EMGS HUB app <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>. Enter the institution and course exactly as in the offer letter, your name exactly as in the two coded lines at the bottom of your passport's data page, and the passport number on the offer letter <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>. EMGS then asks the institution to confirm <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>.
4. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-4"></a>**Pay** in one of three ways <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>:
   - through the institution <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>;
   - by telegraphic transfer (bank-to-bank international transfer): converted to US dollars, plus USD 50 <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>;
   - by Visa or MasterCard on the EMGS site, plus 3% <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>.

   Tuition handed to agents has failed to reach institutions <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>. EMGS's [fee calculator](https://visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my/student-visa/calculate-application-fees) gives, for a new application at a university or private higher education institution:
   - processing: RM1,728 <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - eVAL (electronic Visa Approval Letter, IMI's approval to enter as a student): RM159 <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - medical screening in Malaysia: RM340 (RM350 in Sarawak) <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - Student Pass: RM60 <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - i-Kad (student identity card): RM50, or RM60.60 couriered <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - EMGS health insurance: RM498 to RM644, or a policy through your institution <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>;
   - visa: RM20 <sup>[185](#o185)</sup>.

   8% service tax is added to processing fees <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>. Public-university students may do the medical at their university <sup>[172](#o172)</sup>. If a card payment fails but your bank took the money, email enquiry@emgs.com.my with the bank's confirmation, transaction ID and application number <sup>[184 (2019)](#o184)</sup>.
5. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-5"></a>**Refunds.** The first year's processing and eVAL fees aren't refunded once invoiced; convenience fees and service tax never are <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>. Before the institution decides, you get a full refund if you ask within 90 days of paying <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>, through the online refund form in your account, logged in with the email you applied with; card payments come back by telegraphic transfer <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
6. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-6"></a>**Lodge the RM750 personal bond** (the Bangladeshi rate) with the institution, which signs a bond with Immigration for you; it is returned when you finish or withdraw without breaking immigration rules <sup>[186 (2023)](#o186), [181](#o181)</sup>. The form can be handed in after arrival, for the Student Pass sticker <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How and when does the institution take the money?
   > - What receipt do you get?
   > - How do you claim it back?

7. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-7"></a>**If EMGS asks for more documents,** send them within 14 working days of the return date <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>, or the application closes, and a refund must be asked for within 6 months of closing <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.
8. <a id="step-2-apply-from-bangladesh-item-8"></a>**Wait.** EMGS checks your academic papers, then IMI checks your passport and security and issues the eVAL <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>; EMGS's charter is 14 working days for a complete application <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.

**If you're refused,** you can appeal <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>; the institution's international office handles the appeal or a new application with corrected documents <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. Ask for any refund within 90 days of EMGS's notice <sup>[182](#o182)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the appeal form?
> - What is the appeal fee?
> - What is the appeal deadline?

<a id="step-3-eval-and-visa"></a>

### 5.3 Step 3: eVAL and visa

The eVAL is valid 6 months; you may not enter before it is issued <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>. Then apply on [MyVISA](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/), from outside Malaysia, for a Single Entry Visa under Visa With Reference (VDR), the category needing an approval letter (the eVAL) <sup>[52](#o52), [53](#o53), [187](#o187), [3](#o3), [4](#o4)</sup>. Account and payment work as in [Tourist visa, Step 2: Apply on MyVISA](#step-2-apply-on-myvisa) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>; the fee is RM20 <sup>[185](#o185)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does MyVISA's RM105 processing charge apply to students?

<a id="step-4-check-your-visa-is-real"></a>
