   - a Letter of Good Conduct (police clearance; see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), [Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates));
   - the personal bond form;
   - your highest education certificate <sup>[278](#o278)</sup>;
   - a medical insurance enrolment certificate valid in Malaysia for at least 3 months, covering dependants; it can be handed in after approval, before the pass is issued <sup>[278](#o278)</sup>.

   You'll need, as a freelancer <sup>[279](#o279), [275](#o275)</sup>:

   - the passport, CV, Letter of Good Conduct, bond form, certificate and insurance;
   - up to 3 signed client contracts showing scope, duration and payment terms;
   - 3 months' invoices;
   - proof of payment (PayPal, Stripe, Wise or bank records);
   - 3 months' bank statements above the threshold.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What is the minimum insurance cover?
   > - Which insurers are accepted?
   > - Do Malaysian clients count?

2. <a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass-item-2"></a>**Apply** at [mdec.my/derantau](https://mdec.my/derantau) and pay RM1,080 plus RM540 per dependant online, not refunded <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>. A decision takes 6 to 8 weeks after a complete application; track it at [malaysiadigital.mdec.my](https://malaysiadigital.mdec.my) <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>. No appeal, but you can reapply; no one can guarantee approval <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.

3. <a id="de-rantau-nomad-pass-item-3"></a>**After approval: enter and get the pass endorsed.** The approval letter is valid 6 months, not extendable; finish every step within it <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.

   1. Get an eVISA (a visa applied for online) or, if the letter says so, a VDR (visa with reference) <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>. Apply for the eVISA yourself on MyVISA, with the fees and times in [Tourist visa, Step 2: Apply on MyVISA](#step-2-apply-on-myvisa) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. A tourist or social visit pass can't be converted: leave and re-enter <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.

      > **Need to know**
      > - What does a VDR for DE Rantau cost?
      > - What documents does it need?
      > - How long does it take?

   2. Use a normal Immigration counter, not the automatic gate, to get an entry stamp <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.
   3. Within 1 month, upload on the DE Rantau portal, under your approved application <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>:
      - the personal bond with a RM10 revenue stamp, endorsed by LHDN;
      - all passport pages;
      - the entry stamp;
      - the eVISA or VDR;
      - Malaysian health insurance;
      - an LHDN tax registration slip from mytax.hasil.gov.my.
   4. Pay Immigration RM90 per 3 months or RM360 a year, any multiple-entry visa charge, and a RM750 bond for a Bangladeshi, refunded when the pass expires <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.
   5. Stay in Malaysia until the e-Pass is issued, about 1 week; leaving can cancel the application <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.

<a id="how-long-you-can-stay-3"></a>

### 15.1 How long you can stay

The pass covers Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan; Sabah or Sarawak need a tourist pass <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.

<a id="can-you-work-8"></a>

### 15.2 Can you work?

<a id="if-the-visa-is-refused-8"></a>

### 15.3 If the visa is refused

Contact MDEC's Expats Service Centre (expatctr@mdec.com.my, 03-8315 3106 / 3157) <sup>[277](#o277)</sup> or the form at [mdec.my/contact-us](https://mdec.my/contact-us), enquiry type "Foreign Knowledge Workers" <sup>[280](#o280)</sup>. For a visa or endorsement problem, complain on IMI's SISPAA at [imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) <sup>[260](#o260)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there any official warning about DE Rantau scams?

<a id="scams-9"></a>

### 15.4 Scams

<a id="other-passes"></a>

## 16 Other passes

<a id="residence-pass-talent-rp-t"></a>

### 16.1 Residence Pass-Talent (RP-T)

A 10-year pass for experienced professionals from TalentCorp (Talent Corporation Malaysia, a government agency for skilled workers) <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>. You need <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>:

- 3 consecutive years' work in Malaysia and an Employment Pass with over 3 months left;
- at least RM15,000 basic a month;
- Malaysian income tax paid for 2 years;
- a degree, diploma or professional certificate with 5 years' experience.

The holder can change employer, and the spouse can work without an Employment Pass <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>.

1. Register an account on the RP-T Portal at [rpt.talentcorp.com.my](https://rpt.talentcorp.com.my/), complete the application and upload the documents yourself; no agent may apply for you <sup>[282](#o282)</sup>. Name a local contact: a Malaysian citizen aged 21 or over working and living in Malaysia, with a copy of both sides of their MyKad <sup>[282](#o282)</sup>.
2. Pay TalentCorp online RM540 per person on applying, not refunded; if approved, RM5,400 for you or your spouse and RM4,860 per other dependant; then Immigration RM500 per pass (RM90 for an LTSVP) plus the visa fee <sup>[283](#o283)</sup>.

3. Wait for the pass <sup>[282](#o282)</sup>:
   - approval: 21 working days from a complete application;
   - result: within 10 more;
   - endorsement: 7;
   - delivery by Pos Malaysia: within 7 more.

No appeal; reapply after 1 year <sup>[282](#o282)</sup>.

Questions: RPTCare@myxpats.com.my, 03-7839 7171 <sup>[282](#o282)</sup>; TalentCorp +603 7839 7000, info@talentcorp.com.my <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>.

<a id="malaysia-tech-entrepreneur-programme-mtep"></a>

### 16.2 Malaysia Tech Entrepreneur Programme (MTEP)

For tech founders, through MDEC, at [mdec.my/mtep](https://mdec.my/mtep). You need RM50,000 in personal bank statements, a certificate of good conduct and a sponsor <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>. The two passes <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>:

- **1 year, for new founders** (from RM2,700): the sponsor is a Malaysia Digital Hub or MTEP recognised partner, which signs a personal bond and gives a letter of offer or tenancy agreement.
- **5 years, for experienced founders** (from RM5,400, dependants allowed): the sponsor is a Malaysian sponsor or individual who signs a declaration.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the MTEP processing time?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?
> - Does any nationality restriction apply to Bangladeshis?

<a id="investor-pass"></a>

### 16.3 Investor Pass

For investors working with Malaysia's investment agencies; 6 months, extendable by 6, no dependants <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>. Apply from outside Malaysia on Xpats Gateway ([xpatsgateway.com.my](https://xpatsgateway.com.my/), the platform for professional passes), after the company is fully registered in ESD Online <sup>[285](#o285), [286](#o286)</sup>. Fees <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>:

- RM1,200 (RM1,296 with tax);
- a RM90 pass fee for 6 months;
- RM500 processing;
- the visa fee.

A complete application is processed within 5 working days, and the ePASS appears in Xpats Gateway 1 working day after payment <sup>[285](#o285)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can an Investor Pass refusal be appealed?
> - Does any nationality restriction apply to Bangladeshis?

<a id="premium-visa-programme-pvip"></a>

### 16.4 Premium Visa Programme (PVIP)

IMI's visa of up to 20 years for investors, entrepreneurs and talent, no age limit, allowing work and study <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>. You need offshore income of RM40,000 a month or RM480,000 a year and a RM1 million fixed deposit in a licensed Malaysian bank <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>. Fees:

- participation: RM200,000, plus RM100,000 per dependant <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>, RM2,000 of it paid at online registration <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>;
- the pass: RM2,000 a year <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>.

Dependants <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>:

- a spouse;
- children under 25 (biological, step or adopted);
- disabled children of any age;
- parents and parents-in-law;
- a domestic helper, under the domestic helper rules.

Only IMI-appointed PVIP agencies apply, on [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main) <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>, with your written power of attorney <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>; check the agency is registered with IMI before paying <sup>[287](#o287)</sup>. PVIP years don't count toward permanent residence <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where does IMI publish the list of appointed PVIP agencies?
> - How long does a PVIP application take?
> - Can a refusal be appealed?

<a id="plsxpats"></a>

### 16.5 PLS@XPATS

IMI's online permission for professionals to work up to 30 days on a social visit pass <sup>[288](#o288), [220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>, approved in 3 working days <sup>[88](#o88)</sup>; the company applies as below <sup>[289](#o289)</sup>. Emergency work may be considered by incident, need and policy <sup>[219](#o219)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a PLS@XPATS refusal be appealed?

You'll need (the company) <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>:

- its request letter;
- a copy of every page of your passport, valid 6 months or more;
- its SSM registration;
- the work or project schedule;
- your return ticket.

1. The company applies free at [mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main](https://mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main), without ESD (IMI's Expatriate Services Division) registration; it takes 3 working days, for anyone 18 or over, not blacklisted and not Israeli <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>.
2. You still need the eVISA, and must carry the approval letter or be refused entry for this work <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>.

The approval is valid 30 days from approval for one entry and at most 30 days' work, not extendable, in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan only, with no family pass <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>. Questions: eapp.imi.gov.my enquiry form <sup>[289](#o289)</sup>.

<a id="permanent-residence-entry-permit"></a>

### 16.6 Permanent residence (Entry Permit)

Lets a foreigner live in Malaysia without time limit and work, study and run a business without another pass <sup>[290](#o290), [157](#o157)</sup>; it can be cancelled for harm to public order, security, health or morality <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-apply-1"></a>

#### 16.6.1 Who can apply

A Malaysian citizen's wife, husband or biological child under 18, and "fully foreign" nationals <sup>[290](#o290), [157](#o157)</sup>: investors, experts and professionals in fields recognised by a Malaysian regulating agency, with 5 continuous years on an Employment, Student, Professional Visit or Residence Pass and no break over 6 months <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>. Check your points first at [app.imi.gov.my/epc](https://app.imi.gov.my/epc) <sup>[188](#o188)</sup>. PLKS, MM2H and PVIP holders can't apply <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

You'll need, as originals with copies <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>:

- a passport valid 12 months;
- Form IM.4 (any Immigration office);
- old and new passports (data page and endorsed pages);
- the sponsor's MyKad and declaration;
- 2 white-background passport photos each of you and the sponsor.

Each kind of applicant adds <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>:

- **a spouse:**
  - the information form;
  - the marriage certificate registered in Malaysia;
  - a declaration that the marriage continues;
  - MyKad copies of 2 Malaysian referees;
  - children's birth certificates (and MyKad from 12);
  - proof of address (a tenancy or purchase agreement or a utility bill);
  - the spouse LTSVP deposit receipt;
- **a child under 18:**
  - the foreign parent's passport;
  - the foreign birth certificate;
  - any Malaysian birth registration;
  - the parents' marriage certificates;
  - proof of address;
  - the sponsor's proof of income;
- **a fully foreign applicant:**
  - the information form;
  - 2 referees' MyKad copies.

Copies of Bangladeshi documents (passport, marriage and birth certificates) need attestation by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs or the Bangladesh High Commission, translation into English or Malay, then attestation by a Malaysian mission or Malaysia's Foreign Ministry <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

1. Download IMI's Entry Permit guideline e-book from [imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk/) <sup>[290](#o290)</sup>.
2. Go with your sponsor and all originals to the nearest Immigration office <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>. A fully foreign Bangladeshi pays a RM750 deposit, refundable after 2 years on the permit <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
3. You and the sponsor are interviewed (not a child), and police run a security check <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
4. Six months after the acknowledgement, check at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit); the letter goes to the sponsor <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
5. If approved, go with your sponsor to the nearest Immigration office with their identity card, your passport (valid over 12 months) and the letter to amend your current pass <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
6. Within 30 days, go together to the Visa, Pass and Permit Division at Immigration headquarters, Putrajaya, with the checklist documents, pay RM120 by card, and collect the permit, which never expires <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>. Uncollected within 6 months of the letter without good reason, the approval lapses <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
7. With each new passport, get a new Entry Permit notice stamp at the nearest Immigration office; after more than 6 months abroad it is held while Immigration investigates <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

**If refused:** within 30 days of the refusal letter, write to the Home Minister through the Chief Secretary, Ministry of Home Affairs, Bahagian Hal Ehwal Imigresen, Aras 4, Setia Perkasa 2, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya; questions to 03-8886 3316, 3319 or 3170 or rayuanpermitmasuk@moha.gov.my. You can reapply after 1 year <sup>[157](#o157), [291](#o291)</sup>.

<a id="macs"></a>

### 16.7 MACS

A fast lane at two Johor checkpoints (Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Johor Bahru; Kompleks Sultan Abu Bakar, Tanjung Kupang) for frequent Malaysia–Singapore travellers; it doesn't replace any visa or pass <sup>[292](#o292)</sup>. A foreigner on a long-term pass (Employment, Professional Visit, Student or PLKS) involved in developing Iskandar Malaysia (the Iskandar Regional Development Authority's region) can get a Category B sticker <sup>[292](#o292), [243](#o243)</sup>. To apply <sup>[292](#o292)</sup>:

1. Book on IMI's appointment system at [sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php](https://sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php).
2. Fill in the [MACS form](https://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/pdf-NEW-MACS-FORM-1.pdf).
3. Apply at the MACS counter, Level 1, Wisma Sunway Big Box, Iskandar Puteri, Johor (+607-2333016).

> **Need to know**
> - What is the MACS fee?
> - How long does it take?

<a id="changing-visa-type"></a>

## 17 Changing visa type

- **Visit pass (tourist or family visit) to PLKS (company worker's pass):** You can't switch inside Malaysia. The employer gets the VDR and PLKS while you are outside Malaysia; you enter only after approval <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5)</sup> (see [Work visa: company worker (PLKS)](#work-visa-company-worker-plks)).
- **Visit pass to Longer visit pass:** You can get a longer pass only for illness, an accident or war at home, with proof and a ticket home or onward <sup>[293](#o293)</sup>.
- **Visit pass to DE Rantau Nomad Pass:** You can't switch. A tourist pass can't be converted <sup>[275](#o275)</sup> (see [DE Rantau Nomad Pass](#de-rantau-nomad-pass)).
- **Visit pass to Student Pass:** You can't switch. You must be outside Malaysia when the application is submitted <sup>[52](#o52)</sup> (see [Student visa (Student Pass)](#student-visa-student-pass)).
- **Visit pass to Professional job up to 30 days:** This can only be arranged before you travel. The company gets PLS@Xpats approval (see [PLS@XPATS](#plsxpats)) <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>; without the approval letter you won't be let in for this work <sup>[220 (2022)](#o220)</sup>. For longer work, see [Professional Visit Pass](#professional-visit-pass).
- **Student Pass to Student Pass for a higher level:** You can switch. Apply for Progression (for example bachelor's to master's) within 3 months of finishing; you may stay while it is processed <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.
- **Student Pass to Work pass:** You can switch only through an employer. The employer applies, for example for an Employment Pass <sup>[294](#o294)</sup>. Bangladeshis can't get the Graduate Pass <sup>[53](#o53)</sup>.
- **PLKS to Another employer or sector:** You can move only with the Home Ministry's permission <sup>[9](#o9)</sup> (see [If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)).
- **Dependant Pass to Work:** You can work only on an Employment Pass. The employer applies for an Employment Pass for the dependant <sup>[232](#o232), [249](#o249)</sup> (see [Family of an Employment Pass holder](#family-of-an-employment-pass-holder)).
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, family of an Employment Pass holder to Work or study:** You can work or study only by converting to another pass. An employer applies for an Employment Pass, or you get a Student Pass <sup>[232](#o232)</sup> (see [Employment Pass](#employment-pass); see [Student visa (Student Pass)](#student-visa-student-pass)).
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, spouse of a Malaysian to Work:** You can work. Get the work endorsement in your passport <sup>[194](#o194)</sup> (see [Spouse of a Malaysian citizen](#spouse-of-a-malaysian-citizen)).
- **Any pass, applicant outside Malaysia to Employment Pass:** You can get one. The employer applies for the VDR with the Employment Pass <sup>[235](#o235)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a Student Pass holder switch to an Employment Pass without leaving Malaysia?
> - Can a PLKS worker switch to an Employment Pass without leaving Malaysia?

<a id="asylum"></a>

## 18 Asylum

Asylum gives no right to stay or work in Malaysia <sup>[295](#o295), [296](#o296)</sup>. There is no national asylum system and the Immigration Act has no provision on refugees; UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) decides claims under its own mandate, and its recognition gives no legal immigration status <sup>[295](#o295), [39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Refugees and asylum seekers are treated as undocumented migrants: they can be arrested, detained, fined and deported, and may not work <sup>[296](#o296)</sup>. UNHCR can't grant work permits or stop an arrest; its document is not a visa and at most may reduce the risk of arrest <sup>[296](#o296), [297](#o297)</sup>.

<a id="who-can-get-it"></a>

### 18.1 Who can get it

Refugee status is only for someone who fled persecution or serious harm because of race, religion, nationality, social group or political opinion, or who can't return because of war or serious disorder; someone who left for work or study is a migrant <sup>[295](#o295)</sup>.

<a id="how-to-apply"></a>

### 18.2 How to apply

1. Apply only on UNHCR's My Services portal, reached from help.unhcr.org/malaysia ("Registering with UNHCR"): create a personal account and submit the request; you get a reference number to quote in every contact, and letters are not accepted <sup>[297](#o297)</sup>.
2. Wait to be called for an interview; don't go to the Reception Centre without an appointment <sup>[297](#o297)</sup>. UNHCR deals first with requests from 2022 or earlier and the most vulnerable, and can't say when anyone will be interviewed <sup>[297](#o297), [298](#o298), [295](#o295)</sup>.
3. At the interview you must tell the truth; being coached or misleading UNHCR harms the case <sup>[295](#o295)</sup>.
4. A refusal can be appealed once, within 30 days; then the case closes and UNHCR's help and documents end <sup>[295](#o295)</sup>.

An unregistered pregnant asylum seeker pays full foreigners' fees for antenatal care and childbirth at government facilities <sup>[298](#o298)</sup>.

UNHCR Malaysia, 570 Jalan Bukit Petaling, 50460 Kuala Lumpur, 03 2118 4800; detention hotline +6012 630 5060 <sup>[299 (2024)](#o299)</sup>.

<a id="if-it-is-refused"></a>

### 18.3 If it is refused

<a id="scams-10"></a>

### 18.4 Scams

All UNHCR services are free; anyone asking money for an interview or letters is lying <sup>[297](#o297), [295](#o295)</sup>. Fake UNHCR cards and work permits are sold <sup>[N142](#n142)</sup>. Scan a card's large QR code with the free UNHCR Verify Plus app (Google Play or App Store); "Incompatible QR Code" means it is not genuine <sup>[299 (2024)](#o299)</sup>.

<a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia"></a>

## 19 Having a child in Malaysia

**PLKS workers** may not marry in Malaysia or bring family, and a worker who marries may lose the pass; a pregnancy confirmed at the FOMEMA medical means being sent home (Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Bringing your family) <sup>[9](#o9), [36 (2022)](#o36), [40 (2022)](#o40), [24](#o24), [137](#o137)</sup>.

The steps below are for parents lawfully in Malaysia with family, such as an Employment Pass holder with dependants or the spouse of a Malaysian <sup>[249](#o249), [194](#o194)</sup>.

1. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-1"></a>**Register the birth with JPN** (Jabatan Pendaftaran Negara, the National Registration Department) within 60 days: free, then RM50 as a late registration <sup>[156](#o156), [300](#o300)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>:
   - form JPN.LM01 (original; take it at the JPN office, as the website copy is only a guide);
   - the hospital's birth confirmation form, or a doctor's letter for a home birth;
   - the mother's antenatal card, both passports and the marriage certificate (originals and copies);
   - an AM80 statutory declaration if there was no antenatal care;
   - a utility bill if you live away from the address on your documents.

   1. Find your office at [jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan](https://www.jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan/) (choose your state) <sup>[301](#o301)</sup>. Counters open Monday to Friday 8 am to 4.45 pm in Johor, Melaka, Negeri Sembilan, Pahang, Penang, Perak, Perlis, Selangor, Kuala Lumpur and Putrajaya; in Kedah, Kelantan and Terengganu, Sunday to Wednesday 8 am to 4.45 pm and Thursday 8 am to 3.15 pm <sup>[302](#o302)</sup>.
   2. A parent, the guardian, or someone who knew of or saw the birth hands in the documents and gets the birth certificate <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

   A child born before the parents' marriage was registered takes the mother's status <sup>[303](#o303)</sup>. The child is not Malaysian unless a parent is a citizen or permanent resident at the birth, or the child would otherwise be stateless <sup>[304](#o304)</sup>; JPN decides from the parents' status <sup>[303](#o303)</sup>. Act A1752, not yet in force, will limit this to a citizen parent <sup>[304](#o304)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does a birth in a hospital need a police report before JPN registers it?
   > - How long does JPN take to issue the birth certificate?
   > - How can parents check that a JPN birth certificate is genuine?
   > - Is Act A1752 in force yet?

2. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-2"></a>**Register the birth with Bangladesh,** which Bangladeshi law requires for a child born abroad <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>:
   - the JPN certificate attested by Wisma Putra (Malaysia's foreign ministry);
   - copies of both parents' passports;
   - the marriage certificate (kabinnama), e-apostilled (authenticated online by Bangladesh's foreign ministry), or if older, attested by the Law and then Foreign Ministry in Dhaka;
   - the signed printed application;
   - 2 recent passport-size colour photos on white.

   1. **Attest the JPN certificate** at Wisma Putra's Consular Division (address and hours under [Family visit sponsored by a Malaysian citizen (VDR), Step 1, item 1](#step-1-send-your-papers-to-the-sponsor-item-1)): you or someone with your authorisation letter brings an identity card or passport, and usually collects it the same or next working day <sup>[212 (2023)](#o212)</sup>.
   2. **E-apostille the marriage certificate** through the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' e-Apostille service on MyGov, [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/services?category=sector&data=service-sector-67): up to five days, with a QR code for checking <sup>[306](#o306)</sup>.
   3. **Apply** at [bdris.gov.bd](https://bdris.gov.bd/) (Bangladesh's birth and death registration system) with your permanent Bangladesh address, uploading the child's foreign birth certificate and a parent's passport or NID; without uploads it is rejected <sup>[307](#o307), [305](#o305)</sup>.
   4. The child and both parents take the printed, signed application and documents to the Bangladesh High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[305](#o305), [125](#o125)</sup>, and pay RM5 by card at the consular counter or by deposit to its Maybank account 564427102268 <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>.
   5. **Check it is real** at [everify.bdris.gov.bd](https://everify.bdris.gov.bd/), which shows whether a certificate is digital (17 digits) <sup>[307](#o307)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does Wisma Putra charge to attest the JPN certificate?
   > - What does the e-Apostille of the marriage certificate cost?
   > - How long does the High Commission take to register the birth?
   > - How is the Bangladeshi birth certificate collected?

3. <a id="having-a-child-in-malaysia-item-3"></a>**Get the child a Bangladeshi passport:** a 48-page e-passport valid 5 years for anyone under 18 <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[308 (2024)](#o308), [154](#o154)</sup>:
   - the child in person;
   - the JPN certificate (original, plus a copy attested by JPN);
   - the Bangladeshi birth registration in English showing Malaysia as birthplace, with its online verification copy;
   - both parents' NID numbers, NID copies and online-verified NID copies;
   - copies of both parents' passports and visas;
   - the marriage certificate attested by the Law Ministry, the Foreign Ministry and the High Commission;
   - a photo of each parent;
   - under age 6, a 3R or 4R lab-printed photo on grey.

   1. Book the compulsory appointment with ESKL (Expat Services (Kuala Lumpur) Sdn. Bhd., the High Commission's passport company) at [expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment](https://www.expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment) or 03-92120267 <sup>[308 (2024)](#o308), [154](#o154)</sup>, then go to Southgate Commercial Centre, Level 2, Block E, No 2, Jalan Dua, off Jalan Chan Sow Lin, 55200 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.
   2. Pay RM545 (RM485 government fee plus RM60 ESKL charge) <sup>[308 (2024)](#o308), [154](#o154)</sup>; a Maybank cash deposit goes to 564427102268 with the child's name on the receipt <sup>[308 (2024)](#o308)</sup>.
   3. Track it at [epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status) with the Application or Online Registration ID and the child's date of birth <sup>[309](#o309)</sup>; questions to the passport WhatsApp, +60104303020 <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long does the child's passport take to issue?
   > - How is the child's passport collected?
   > - How can parents spot a fake passport agent?

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

## 20 Sending money home

**Use a licensed remitter, never hundi** (sending through an unlicensed agent) <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>. In 2022 Bangladesh Bank was reported to warn that hundi is a punishable offence, and money received in about 480 bKash and Nagad agent accounts suspended over hundi could not be cashed <sup>[N77 (2022)](#n77)</sup>. A money-sending shop must display its Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM, the central bank) licence or its certificate as a licensed company's agent; without one it is not authorised <sup>[310](#o310)</sup>. Licensed remitters such as MoneyGram, Western Union, NBL Money Transfer, Agrani Remittance House, Merchantrade and CBL Money Transfer cost about 5% to 5.5% to send RM610; banks about 9% to 16% <sup>[311](#o311)</sup>.

You'll need:
- the receiver's bKash number (a Bangladeshi mobile-money account) and full name as registered with bKash <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>;
- for Touch 'n Go eWallet (a Malaysian wallet app), a passport valid 12 more months and a valid work visa <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>.

1. <a id="sending-money-home-item-1"></a>**Check the licence** in BNM's List of MSB (money services business) Licensees, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees), which also lists head offices and branches, or for an agent shop the List of Approved MSB Agents, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents) <sup>[312](#o312), [313](#o313), [314](#o314)</sup>.
2. <a id="sending-money-home-item-2"></a>**Send.** Ria, CBL Money Transfer, Merchantrade, NBL Money Transfer, WorldRemit, Wise, Touch 'n Go eWallet (no Malaysian bank account needed), Western Union, MoneyGram and others send to bKash <sup>[38](#o38), [N190](#n190)</sup>. At a partner bank, exchange house or agent, give the bKash number and registered name and hand over the cash <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>. Up to Tk 250,000 per transfer, any number of times <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>.
3. <a id="sending-money-home-item-3"></a>**The family** gets the government's 2.5% incentive on legal remittances <sup>[38](#o38), [N76](#n76)</sup> and can cash out at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs for Tk 7 per thousand <sup>[38](#o38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the remittance receipt show, such as a tracking reference?
> - How long does money sent to bKash from Malaysia take to arrive?

<a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive"></a>

### 20.1 If the money doesn't arrive

1. Complain to the remitter's or bank's Complaints Unit by phone (note the officer's name, date and time), then in writing with your name, account number and receipt copies; keep a copy. BNM won't take a complaint that skipped this step <sup>[313](#o313), [315](#o315)</sup>.
2. No reply in 14 days: go to BNMLINK, BNM's customer centre, at [bnmlink.bnm.gov.my](https://bnmlink.bnm.gov.my/) or Ground Floor, D Block, Bank Negara Malaysia, Jalan Dato' Onn, 50480 Kuala Lumpur (9 am to 5 pm, Monday to Friday), or call BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 <sup>[315](#o315), [313](#o313)</sup>. For a bank, also ABMConnect (Association of Banks in Malaysia), 1-300-88-9980 <sup>[313](#o313)</sup>.

Report hundi businesses to BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 <sup>[313](#o313)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BNMLINK take complaints against a licensed remitter?
> - Which Bangladesh Bank circular sets this year's 2.5% incentive?
> - How can a worker without a valid pass send money home legally?

<a id="saving-and-pension-in-bangladesh"></a>

### 20.2 Saving and pension in Bangladesh

<a id="going-home-without-valid-papers"></a>

## 21 Going home without valid papers

Without a valid pass you can go home under Malaysia's repatriation programme, PRM 2.0 (Program Repatriasi Migran 2.0), 1 May 2026 to 31 May 2027, at Immigration offices in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan <sup>[316](#o316), [47](#o47)</sup>, paying a compound (a fixed fine instead of prosecution) <sup>[47](#o47), [N191](#n191)</sup>. Otherwise it is arrest, detention in an immigration depot until removal, and deportation (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39), [N192](#n192)</sup>.

PRM 2.0 only lets you leave; no legalisation programme is open (see [What running away or an expired pass does to you](#what-running-away-or-an-expired-pass-does-to-you)) <sup>[316](#o316), [N88](#n88)</sup>. Anyone but an employer offering to get you a pass is breaking the law (see [Going home without valid papers, Scams](#scams-11)) <sup>[87 (2021)](#o87)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Will Malaysia open a new legalisation round, now that Bangladesh asked it in August 2026 to register undocumented Bangladeshis <sup>[N193](#n193)</sup>?

<a id="help-from-bangladesh"></a>

### 21.1 Help from Bangladesh

A close relative can ask the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment to help bring a worker home; it acts through the High Commission, the Welfare Board or BMET, free, within 7 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>:
- proof the person is a migrant worker;
- their workplace and home address in Malaysia;
- the recruiting agency's name and address;
- the employer's mobile number.

1. Write to the Ministry, Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000, or secretary@probashi.gov.bd; phone +88-02-41030444 <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.
2. No reply: the grievance officer, Joint Secretary (Admin and Finance), 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, has 30 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Ministry acknowledge a family's application?
> - How can the family check the application's progress?

<a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit"></a>

### 21.2 Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit

With a valid passport, go to [Step 2: Buy a ticket home](#step-2-buy-a-ticket-home). If it has expired or been lost or damaged, get a Travel Permit (a one-way "out pass" home) from the Bangladesh High Commission <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>.

You'll need <sup>[305](#o305)</sup>:
- a copy of your old passport;
- a copy of your NID or online birth registration;
- two colour photos;
- your phone number.

Without an NID, a citizenship certificate from your UNO (Upazila Nirbahi Officer, the sub-district's chief officer), or the Deputy Commissioner for Cox's Bazar, emailed to the High Commission's labour wing at counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com and fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.

1. Go yourself (no one can go for you) to the High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur, on an office day, 9:30 am to 1 pm <sup>[305](#o305), [125](#o125), [130](#o130)</sup>.
2. Pay RM38, the only fee, to the High Commission itself, by card at the consular counter or by deposit to Maybank account 564427102268 <sup>[317](#o317), [305](#o305)</sup>.
3. Collect it, usually the same day after 4 pm; it is valid 3 months <sup>[130](#o130), [305](#o305)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker in Malaysia get the UNO citizenship certificate?
> - What does the UNO certificate cost?
> - How long does the UNO certificate take?
> - Does the Travel Permit take 1 to 2 working days rather than the same day, which matters before you date your ticket <sup>[318 (2023)](#o318)</sup>?

**If it fails:** consular section +60326040949, or Minister (Labour) Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +601 2290 3252 <sup>[125](#o125), [107](#o107)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-lose-your-passport-in-malaysia"></a>

#### 21.2.1 If you lose your passport in Malaysia

You'll need <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>:
- the passport number or a copy;
- your new passport or Travel Permit ([Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit));
- a police report;
- a plane, bus or ferry ticket out dated within 7 days.

1. Report the loss at any police station, orally to the officer (who writes it and reads it back) or on form Pol. 55, free at the counter; any report must be in Malay or English; buy a certified copy for RM2, at once or within 3 days <sup>[319](#o319)</sup>.
2. Report at once, yourself, without agents, to the Enforcement Division at your state Immigration office ([Step 3: Go to Immigration](#step-3-go-to-immigration)) <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.
3. Pay RM100 for a Special Pass <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does Immigration take to issue the Special Pass after a lost passport?

<a id="step-2-buy-a-ticket-home"></a>

### 21.3 Step 2: Buy a ticket home

You'll need: your passport or Travel Permit, and the fare.

1. Buy a confirmed ticket to Bangladesh dated 5 to 14 days after the day you go to Immigration <sup>[47](#o47), [N194](#n194), [N195](#n195)</sup>.
2. **Check it is real** on the airline's website with the six-character PNR (booking reference) and your last name; on Biman, "Manage Booking" <sup>[N137](#n137)</sup>.
3. **If you'll miss the date,** change it beforehand: on Biman, Manage Booking, Exchange, new date, pay any fare difference or fee <sup>[N137](#n137)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where can a worker buy the ticket home safely?
> - What is the usual fare from Malaysia to Bangladesh?
> - Does Immigration accept a ticket whose date was changed?

<a id="step-3-go-to-immigration"></a>

### 21.4 Step 3: Go to Immigration

You'll need <sup>[N194](#n194), [47](#o47)</sup>:
- your original passport or Travel Permit;
- a copy of the photo page;
- a copy of your last Malaysian pass;
- old and new passports if you have both;
- the confirmed ticket;
- a debit or credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet with enough for the compound.

1. Go yourself, without appointment, to one of the 14 Immigration Enforcement offices (headquarters and state offices); no one can apply for you <sup>[316](#o316), [47](#o47), [N194](#n194), [N195](#n195)</sup>. Contacts: [imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami/).
   - Putrajaya (headquarters): No. 15, Tingkat 1-7, Blok 2G4 (Menara & Podium), Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya, 03-8000 8000 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Kuala Lumpur: Kompleks KDN, No 69, Jalan Sri Hartamas 1, off Jalan Duta, 50550 Kuala Lumpur, 03-62057400 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Selangor: Tingkat 2, Kompleks PKNS, 40550 Shah Alam, 03-5650 0000 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Negeri Sembilan: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Dato' Abdul Kadir, 70000 Seremban, 06-7620000 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Johor: Blok 1, Kompleks KDN, Taman Setia Tropika, 81200 Johor Bahru, 07-2338400 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Penang: Jalan Kelasah, 13700 Seberang Jaya, 04-3973011 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Perak: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Meru Utama, Bandar Meru Raya, 30020 Ipoh, 05-5017100 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Pahang: Kompleks KDN, Bandar Indera Mahkota, 25200 Kuantan, 09-5717999 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Melaka: Kompleks KDN, Jalan Seri Negeri, Hang Tuah Jaya, 75450 Ayer Keroh, 06-2322662 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Kedah: Bangunan KDN, Bandar Mu'adzam Shah, 06550 Alor Setar, 04-7333302 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Perlis: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Wawasan, 01000 Kangar, 04-9762636 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Terengganu: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Sultan Ismail, 20200 Kuala Terengganu, 09-6221424 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Kelantan: Kompleks Imigresen Negeri Kelantan, Jalan Hospital, 15000 Kota Bharu, 09-7482120 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
   - Labuan: Unit E002, Tingkat 1, Aras Podium, Kompleks Ujana Kewangan, Jalan Merdeka, 87008 Labuan, 087-425 307 <sup>[214](#o214)</sup>.
2. Arrive early: at Seremban, queues start before the 8 am opening <sup>[N196](#n196)</sup>.
3. Pay the compound by debit card, credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet only <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>:
   - RM500 for entering or staying without a valid pass or for overstaying;
   - RM300 for breaking pass conditions.

   Plus RM20 for the Special Pass (Pas Khas, a pass to leave within a set time); under-18s pay only the RM20 <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>.
4. Leave on your ticket within the Special Pass's time <sup>[47](#o47), [130](#o130)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are the opening hours of the other Immigration offices?

**Who can't use PRM 2.0** <sup>[N197](#n197), [N198](#n198)</sup>:
- anyone who registered in an earlier programme but didn't leave;
- anyone blacklisted by Immigration;
- anyone wanted or under an arrest warrant.

An overstaying spouse of a Malaysian goes instead to Immigration's Visa, Pass and Permit Division <sup>[N198](#n198)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do these rules still apply in the PRM 2.0 extension to May 2027?

<a id="scams-11"></a>

#### 21.4.1 Scams

PRM is run by Immigration alone, with no agents; don't pay any broker or anyone asking money for special treatment <sup>[N195](#n195), [N194](#n194)</sup>.

**If something goes wrong:**

- PRM line, 03-8880 1269 <sup>[316](#o316)</sup>.
- The High Commission's labour wing ([Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit), "If it fails"), or Minister (Labour) Md. Siddiqur Rahman, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, +603 2604 0934 <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>.
- Family in Bangladesh: Probashi call centre (Wage Earners' Welfare Board), 16135 free, +8809610102030 from abroad, 24 hours <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.
- Tenaganita (see Free help in Malaysia): +603 7770 3671; emergency +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 <sup>[N158](#n158)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 21.5 If you are detained

With a valid passport a detainee needs only a ticket, and the depot takes them to the airport and clears them <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>; High Commission officers visit prisons as well as detention camps <sup>[N151](#n151)</sup>. The rest (Travel Permit without a passport, family visits and contact): see [After arrest](#after-arrest).

<a id="bans-after-you-leave"></a>

### 21.6 Bans after you leave

Immigration's Director General can bar a person for a set period or for life, and a person whose pass is cancelled is removed and barred <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Deported people are blacklisted for 1 to 5 years by offence, or for life in serious cases, and the blacklist records fingerprints (BioSL) <sup>[N199](#n199), [N200](#n200), [N108](#n108), [N128 (2017)](#n128), [N201 (2024)](#n201)</sup>. A ban or pass cancellation can be appealed within 7 days by a written petition, with the grounds in detail, to the Minister of Home Affairs <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>: Blok D1 & D2, Kompleks D, Pusat Pentadbiran Kerajaan Persekutuan, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 8000 <sup>[320](#o320)</sup>. Removal for an expired or cancelled pass can't be appealed <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>. Returning unlawfully after removal can bring a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail and whipping <sup>[39 (2006)](#o39)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long is the ban after leaving under PRM 2.0?
> - Does the petition go to the Ministry of Home Affairs directly or through Immigration?
> - What does the petition cost?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 22 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

PERKESO (SOCSO, see [After you arrive, item 7](#after-you-arrive-item-7)) pays for injuries, disability and death in Malaysia, and the labour wing chases employers for compensation <sup>[321](#o321), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>. In Bangladesh the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the Welfare Board; Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka) pays grants and handles the insurance bought at BMET clearance, which Jiban Bima Corporation, the state life insurer, pays out <sup>[322](#o322), [323](#o323), [324](#o324), [N202](#n202)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1"></a>

### 22.1 If you are injured or fall ill

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-1"></a>**Get treatment.** Free at a government clinic or hospital, or at a PERKESO panel clinic (a private clinic PERKESO appoints), which after a workplace accident checks your cover itself on PERKESO's SEHATi system <sup>[321](#o321), [325](#o325)</sup>. Panel clinics are listed by state (Selangor: perkeso.gov.my/images/Klinik_Panel/Senarai_Klinik/SENARAI_KLINIK_PANEL_SELANGOR.pdf); PERKESO's line is 1-300-22-8000 <sup>[326](#o326), [325](#o325)</sup>. Non-panel clinic costs can be claimed back from PERKESO <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>. At government hospitals SPIKPA covers you without a deposit (see [After you arrive, item 7](#after-you-arrive-item-7)), and sick leave must be reported within 48 hours (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[144](#o144), [33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where is a current list of PERKESO panel clinics for each state?

2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-2"></a>**Make sure the accident is reported and the claim filed.** The employer must report it to PERKESO at once, within 30 days at the latest, or face a fine or prosecution <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. Covered: accidents at work, between home and work and during an emergency, work diseases, and since 1 June 2026 accidents outside work <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>. The claim is on Form 34-PA (perkeso.gov.my/images/borang/Borang34/290626-BORANG_34-PA.pdf); the worker or dependant signs as claimant, and the employer fills in its sections if you still work there <sup>[328](#o328), [329](#o329)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>:
   - Form 34-PA;
   - passport and PLKS copies;
   - your medical or sick-leave certificate;
   - for a road accident, the original police report or a copy certified by the issuing police station.

   Quote your 12-digit FWSS (Foreign Workers Social Security) number in every dealing with PERKESO <sup>[141 (2019)](#o141)</sup>.

   - Where and when: hand it in at a PERKESO counter and keep the stamped slip, or post it to the nearest PERKESO office (the form asks which office you want to deal with); head office: Menara PERKESO, 281 Jalan Ampang, 50538 Kuala Lumpur <sup>[327](#o327), [329](#o329), [330](#o330)</sup>. File within 12 months of the benefit falling due (the appeal board can extend this for good reason); incomplete, unclear or late claims can be rejected <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331), [327](#o327)</sup>.
   - Cost: free; don't use agents or middlemen who charge <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. A false claim can bring up to 2 years' jail, a fine of up to RM10,000 or both <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>. Progress: 1 300 22 8000 or perkeso@perkeso.gov.my <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>.
   - If PERKESO refuses or underpays, appeal to the Social Security Appellate Board within 3 years; a Medical Board's disability assessment goes to the Appellate Medical Board <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does PERKESO accept Form 34-PA without the employer's sections when the employer refuses to sign?
   > - How does a worker find the nearest PERKESO branch's address?
   > - How long does PERKESO take to decide a claim?
   > - How is an appeal filed to the Social Security Appellate Board?

3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-3"></a>**What you can get.**
   - Temporary Disablement Benefit for medical leave of at least 4 days, counting the accident day <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>.
   - For permanent disability, assessed by a Medical Board in Malaysia: up to 20% is paid as a lump sum; above 20%, one fifth can be taken as a lump sum and the rest monthly for life <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>.
   - A 100% disabled worker needing constant care gets RM500 a month more, and PERKESO pays for physiotherapy, artificial limbs, wheelchairs and similar aids <sup>[321](#o321), [35](#o35)</sup>.
   - A worker certified invalid who hasn't paid Invalidity Scheme contributions long enough gets a one-time Invalidity Grant of both sides' contributions with interest <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>.

   Payments can go to a bank outside Malaysia: give the account holder, bank, address, branch, account number and SWIFT code on the form, with a bank verification letter <sup>[329](#o329), [327](#o327)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-4"></a>**If the employer won't report it or pay you while you are off work,** file a case at the nearest Labour Department office (offices by state at jtksm.mohr.gov.my/ms/hubungi-kami/alamat-ibu-pejabat-dan-cawangan) with your payslips, contract and attendance record, and give a copy to the labour wing (counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +60 12-290 3252), which pursues compensation claims <sup>[332](#o332), [131 (2024)](#o131), [107](#o107), [N156](#n156)</sup>. The Labour Court works as in route 3 (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)); if the employer ignores its Order, the Labour Department can seize and sell its property, take the money from its bank or prosecute it <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97)</sup>. Sample message to the labour wing: "I am [name], passport [number], PLKS with [employer, address]. I was injured at work on [date] at [place]. My employer has not reported it to PERKESO. Please help me claim." Tenaganita and the Bar Council's Legal Aid Centres help with cases (see Free help in Malaysia) <sup>[N147](#n147), [N161](#n161)</sup>; the ILO (International Labour Organization) does not take complaints from individual workers <sup>[333](#o333)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does Tenaganita charge workers for its help?

5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-5"></a>**Going home injured.** On your or your family's application, with proof of BMET clearance or Board membership and a passport copy, the Welfare Board asks the High Commission to bring you home <sup>[334](#o334)</sup>. The Board's ambulance at Dhaka and Chattogram airports is free on request <sup>[335](#o335)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-6"></a>**Treatment money in Bangladesh.** A returnee with BMET clearance or Board membership who is injured, sick or disabled can get up to Tk 2 lakh <sup>[336](#o336)</sup>. Within 1 year of return, the family applies to the Board's Director General via its web portal, the district probashi kallyan (migrant welfare) centre or the Board <sup>[336](#o336), [322](#o322)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[336](#o336), [322](#o322)</sup>:
   - medical reports and prescriptions;
   - a bank statement;
   - a passport copy and a copy of its arrival stamp;
   - proof of clearance or membership;
   - one 6R (6 by 8 inch) colour photo of the worker.

   A Board committee checks the case by WhatsApp video call and recommends an amount; free, up to 60 working days after all papers are in <sup>[336](#o336), [322](#o322)</sup>.
7. <a id="if-you-are-injured-or-fall-ill-1-item-7"></a>**Insurance.** It pays Tk 10 lakh for total permanent disability and 10% to 90% of that for partial permanent disability <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>; see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance).

<a id="if-a-worker-dies"></a>

### 22.2 If a worker dies

These steps are for the family in Bangladesh.

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-1"></a>**Ask the Welfare Board to bring the body home.** Call 16135 (from abroad +8809610102030) <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. Then apply in one of these ways, even if the worker had no BMET clearance or Board membership <sup>[N203](#n203)</sup>:
   - on myGov (the government's online services portal), service "Application to bring home a migrant worker's body", https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638167134, with a myGov account (register with your mobile number at idp-v2.live.mygov.bd/registration), the worker's passport and any clearance or membership; a tracking number comes by SMS and you can follow the case on myGov; free; up to 35 working days <sup>[337](#o337)</sup>; or
   - to the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, which also handles undocumented workers, describing what happened and the cause of death, with proof the person was a migrant worker (passport, work permit, BMET smart card, Malaysian ID, death certificate); free <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

   Sample: "My [relation], [name], passport [number], worked for [employer, phone] in [town], Malaysia, and died on [date]. We want the body brought home to [district]. The receiver is [name, phone]."
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-2"></a>**Home or burial in Malaysia.** The Board passes the family's choice to the High Commission, and the family sends the High Commission a claim or consent letter naming the receiver and their phone number <sup>[338](#o338), [339](#o339), [131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. For burial in Malaysia, apply on myGov with the service "Application for permission to bury a migrant worker's body in the destination country", https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638246958, free, 3 working days <sup>[340](#o340)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[340](#o340)</sup>:
   - the worker's passport or visa copy;
   - any clearance or membership;
   - the district office's investigation report;
   - a certificate signed by all heirs on the union parishad (rural local council) chairman's letterhead.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-3"></a>**The Malaysian side.** The employer writes to the High Commission, appoints a funeral company and sends its letters, the insurance claim and compensation letters, the hospital letter, the death certificate, the Malaysian police report and the passport <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. It pays the funeral and repatriation <sup>[36 (2022)](#o36)</sup>. For its no-objection certificate (NOC) the High Commission also needs <sup>[341 (2023)](#o341)</sup>:
   - the employer's PERKESO death report;
   - for an abnormal death (hanging, fall, accident, murder), a police report with NOC;
   - for an undocumented worker, the original passport (or a police report of its loss), the receiver's details, a police report with NOC, the original death certificate, the funeral company's letter and the employer's name and phone.

   The Board forwards the request within 3 to 5 working days, and the labour wing takes about 30 <sup>[337](#o337)</sup>. An undocumented worker's body can take much longer to come home <sup>[N203](#n203)</sup>.

   If there is no employer or it refuses, the Welfare Board pays <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124), [131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>. PERKESO repays whoever paid (a person, company or the High Commission) up to RM4,500 of repatriation, even if the death isn't otherwise covered, on its PPJ (Funeral Repatriation Cost Claim) form, on which the employer states whether it paid; hand it in at a PERKESO counter or post it, with <sup>[321](#o321), [342 (2022)](#o342), [343 (2020)](#o343)</sup>:
   - a certified copy of the death certificate and of the claimant's ID or company registration;
   - the original receipt;
   - bank proof;
   - proof the body was sent home (the Ministry of Health's export permit or the airline's air waybill).

   Malaysian authorities trace the family through the permanent address and emergency contact in the worker's passport, so write a full name and a working phone number in the passport's emergency contact field; one body waited 15 days in a Kuala Lumpur morgue, at RM75 a day, because that field held only "0088" <sup>[313](#o313), [N204](#n204)</sup>. If no family is found, the worker may be buried in Malaysia as an unclaimed person <sup>[N204](#n204)</sup>.

   Death papers usually give the cause as illness or natural death <sup>[N203](#n203)</sup>; migrants say deaths are recorded as stroke or heart attack to avoid workplace-accident compensation, and Bangladesh doesn't check the cause <sup>[N203](#n203), [N205](#n205)</sup>. If the family doubts it, apply to the Welfare Board for legal help abroad: myGov https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638179893, at the Board, or through the expatriate welfare branch of the Deputy Commissioner's office (the district administration) or the DEMO, with proof of clearance or membership and a passport or travel permit copy with arrival and departure stamps; free; sent to the labour wing within 1 to 2 days; up to 20 working days <sup>[344](#o344), [345](#o345)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a family ask the Malaysian police for a post-mortem or inquest?
   > - How does the family make that request?

4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-4"></a>**At the airport.** The Board's desk gives a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial, collectable within 3 months, and a free ambulance <sup>[346](#o346), [335](#o335)</sup>. You'll need <sup>[347](#o347), [346](#o346)</sup>:
   - the worker's passport copy;
   - a heirship (warish) certificate from the union parishad chairman, mayor or city councillor;
   - the NID of the wife, father or mother;
   - the death certificate;
   - the NOC;
   - the original air waybill;
   - proof of clearance or membership;
   - a photo and NID or passport copy of the named receiver, and of the collector if different.
5. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-5"></a>**The Tk 3 lakh Welfare Board grant,** for the family of a worker with clearance or membership who died in Malaysia, the country of the clearance <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>. If the family took the airport cheque, no application is needed: the Board writes asking for the papers; after burial in Malaysia, apply within 1 year of the death to the Board's Director General or through the district office <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>. The grant goes to the spouse, children and parents; for an unmarried worker with no living parents, to full siblings <sup>[323](#o323)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[323](#o323), [322](#o322)</sup>:
   - a family-members certificate on the letterhead of the union parishad chairman, mayor or city councillor, countersigned by the UNO or, in a city corporation, the regional executive officer;
   - an indemnity, undertaking and authorisation on Tk 400 non-judicial stamp paper, countersigned the same way (samples on wewb.gov.bd);
   - the NOC or death certificate copy;
   - a bank certificate or original statement for the receiver's account with the routing number (minor children's own account where they apply);
   - each family member's colour photo, attested by the chairman and the district welfare centre;
   - the worker's passport copy.

   Paid by bank transfer (BEFTN), with an SMS, within 60 working days of all papers reaching the Board <sup>[323](#o323), [322](#o322)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-6"></a>**The insurance:** Tk 10 lakh for a death within 5 years of the smart card date, for workers who left with clearance on or after 1 January 2023 <sup>[348](#o348), [19](#o19)</sup>. Cover for those who left between 19 December 2019 and 31 December 2022 lasted 2 years and has ended <sup>[N204](#n204)</sup> (see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance)).

   > **Need to know**
   > - Are workers who left between 1 January and 11 March 2023 covered, since BMET's leaflet gives the start as 12 March 2023 <sup>[19](#o19)</sup>?

7. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-7"></a>**Malaysian compensation.** PERKESO pays:
   - a Dependants' Benefit for a work-related death <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>;
   - a funeral benefit against receipts of up to RM7,500 for burial in Bangladesh (up to RM4,500 to whoever paid repatriation, RM3,000 to the dependants) or up to RM3,000 for burial in Malaysia <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>;
   - if the worker died in Malaysia before 60 from any cause, a Survivors' Pension of at least RM550 a month, if Invalidity Scheme contributions were paid for at least 24 of the 40 consecutive months before the claim or two-thirds of the months since they became due <sup>[321](#o321)</sup>.

   Claim within 12 months <sup>[331 (2019)](#o331)</sup>. You'll need <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>:
   - Form 34-PA;
   - the worker's passport and work pass copies;
   - the death certificate or burial permit;
   - proof of relationship (marriage certificate, widow's ID and children's birth certificates; or, with no wife or child, the worker's birth certificate and parents' IDs);
   - bank proof;
   - Bangladeshi papers translated into English by an accredited translator and certified by the High Commission or a Bangladesh authority <sup>[327](#o327)</sup>.

   For an accident, the Labour Department sends its Form H to the heirs, who fill it in and return it through the High Commission <sup>[131 (2024)](#o131)</sup>.

   How it works: when the High Commission reports that compensation may be due, the Welfare Board asks the family for a power of attorney and the papers, with free help from its legal cell, sends them to the High Commission and follows the case; the labour wing recovers compensation and unpaid wages, and the Board pays them to the heirs within 60 working days of having the papers and the money <sup>[349](#o349), [322](#o322), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>. The family can also ask the Ministry (aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd), which writes to the High Commission and the Board within 7 working days <sup>[100](#o100)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long does PERKESO take to pay a death claim filed through the High Commission?

<a id="if-you-lose-the-job"></a>

### 22.3 If you lose the job

1. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-1"></a>**The employer must** tell the Labour Department within 30 days <sup>[33 (2023)](#o33)</sup>. Under the MoU's standard contract it gives 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages, pays all money owed and pays your fare home unless you broke the contract <sup>[40 (2022)](#o40)</sup>. It sends you home on a Check-Out Memo <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>; with under 4 weeks' notice you may stay in its housing up to 4 weeks <sup>[150 (2021)](#o150)</sup>. You can't simply move to another employer ([If you want to change employer](#if-you-want-to-change-employer)) <sup>[41 (2024)](#o41)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-2"></a>**To claim money owed,** go to the Labour Court (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[97 (2017)](#o97)</sup> with:
   - your passport;
   - the workplace's name and address;
   - payslips and income details;
   - appointment letter and terms of service;
   - any termination letter.
3. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-3"></a>**If you were dismissed without just cause,** file a written representation to the Director General of Industrial Relations, by hand at the Industrial Relations Department (JPP) office nearest the workplace, within 60 days of the dismissal or the end of the notice period, and keep the acknowledgement; union membership isn't needed and you can represent yourself <sup>[350 (2021)](#o350), [N206](#n206)</sup>. JPP tries to settle, then sends the case to the Industrial Court, which can award compensation only if you asked for reinstatement <sup>[350 (2021)](#o350), [N206](#n206)</sup>. A letter showing the case is pending usually gets you a Special Pass (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid), route 3) <sup>[N82 (2021)](#n82)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How does a worker find the address of the JPP office nearest the workplace?
   > - Can the representation be filed online?
   > - Is there a fee to file it?

4. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-4"></a>**Insurance:** coming home within 6 months of going after losing the job pays Tk 50,000 <sup>[19](#o19), [348](#o348)</sup>; see [Claiming the insurance](#claiming-the-insurance).

<a id="claiming-the-insurance"></a>

### 22.4 Claiming the insurance

1. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-1"></a>**Call 16135** (+8809610102030 from abroad); the Welfare Board helps claim from Jiban Bima Corporation <sup>[76](#o76), [324](#o324)</sup>.
2. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-2"></a>**Apply.** For a death, nothing is needed if the body came through the airport desk; after burial in Malaysia or death after return, apply at portal.wewb.gov.bd or at the Board <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>. For disability or a lost job, apply to the Board's welfare section <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>:
   - disability: Deputy Director, +88 02 48317085, 01321-158354, dd.welfare2@wewb.gov.bd;
   - lost job: Assistant Director, 01712-107301, jahanwewb@gmail.com.
3. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-3"></a>**You'll need:**
   - proof of clearance or membership and a passport copy <sup>[324](#o324)</sup>, with departure and arrival stamps for a lost job <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>;
   - for disability, medical papers and a 6R colour photo of the disabled part of the body <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>;
   - for a death, the papers in [If a worker dies, item 5: The Tk 3 lakh Welfare Board grant](#if-a-worker-dies-item-5).

   Free; 30 working days <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does Jiban Bima Corporation pay the insurance by bank transfer or cheque?

4. <a id="claiming-the-insurance-item-4"></a>**If it stalls** (families have waited months <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup>), complain in this order <sup>[322](#o322)</sup>:
   1. the Board's grievance officer, +88 02 55138267, 01684-501891, azizwelfare@gmail.com (30 working days);
   2. the Ministry's Joint Secretary (Administration), +88 02 41030261, jsadmin@probashi.gov.bd (20 working days);
   3. grs.gov.bd, the Cabinet Division's grievance system.

   The Ministry's complaint cell also takes email at minister.expat@gmail.com <sup>[196](#o196)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 23 Coming home

- **Leaving Malaysia with cash.** You may carry any amount of foreign currency, and ringgit up to the equivalent of USD 10,000; above USD 10,000 in total, declare it on Customs form No. 22, available before the customs checkpoint, or face a fine of up to RM1 million, up to a year in jail or both <sup>[351](#o351), [352 (2009)](#o352)</sup>.
- **Customs in Bangladesh.**
  - Duty-free: 65 kg of baggage, 2 used phones and 1 new phone a year, and once a year, if declared, 100 g of gold or 200 g of silver jewellery (no more than 12 pieces of one type) <sup>[353](#o353)</sup>.
  - Once a year you may bring a declared gold bar up to 117 g (10 tola; duty Tk 5,000 per tola) or silver bar up to 234 g; a bigger or hidden bar is confiscated <sup>[353](#o353), [354](#o354)</sup>.
  - No alcohol on a Bangladeshi passport <sup>[353](#o353)</sup>.
  - Declare foreign currency over USD 10,000 on the FMJ form <sup>[354](#o354)</sup>.
  - Customs emergency line: +88 01319-056591 <sup>[354](#o354)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - May BMET card holders who have been abroad at least 6 months bring 2 new phones a year duty-free?

- **Airport help.** The Welfare Board's desks help with boarding cards, customs and lost luggage, take complaints of abuse or cheating, and send sick returnees to hospital by ambulance; Dhaka desk +8802 8901040, 01404 413388 <sup>[347](#o347), [335](#o335)</sup>. Women returning within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000 cash <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
- **A place to stay.**
  - The Wage Earners' Centre (Lonjonipara, Borua, Khilkhet, Dhaka-1229, near Shahjalal airport): Tk 100 admission, Tk 200 a night; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd or in person with proof of clearance or membership, a passport copy and the ticket; 01310 350555, 01754 715720 (24 hours) <sup>[355](#o355)</sup>.
  - The Probashi Lounge at Shahjalal airport gives free wifi and phone calls and food at up to 30% off when you show proof of clearance or membership <sup>[356](#o356)</sup>.
- **If your family is threatened or its property seized,** apply to the Welfare Board for legal help (myGov https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1638181581, the Board, the expatriate welfare branch of the Deputy Commissioner's office or the DEMO) with the worker's passport copy and any clearance or membership; free, up to 3 working days; the Board writes to the local administration, such as the Deputy Commissioner or Superintendent of Police <sup>[357](#o357), [358](#o358), [124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.
- **A business loan.** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the government bank for migrants) lends returnees who start a project up to Tk 50 lakh, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral, at 9% simple interest over up to 10 years; apply at the PKB branch for the business's area on its free form <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>.

  You'll need <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>:
  - 3 passport photos;
  - passport and NID copies;
  - a certificate of your present and permanent address from the pourashava (municipality) or union parishad;
  - a current trade licence;
  - project details with 2 years' income and expenses;
  - the premises' lease or ownership papers;
  - a declaration of your own investment and other loans;
  - copies of your return papers;
  - 3 signed cheque leaves from the business account;
  - a guarantor (a relative or anyone able to repay) with 2 photos, ID copy, address certificate, a clean CIB report (Bangladesh Bank's credit report showing no unpaid loans) and 3 signed cheque leaves.

  Fees: 0.50% processing and 0.50% documentation, each capped at Tk 15,000, each plus VAT, paid when you take the loan; no service charge <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>. Decision within 15 working days of a complete application <sup>[359](#o359)</sup>. Helpline 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[32](#o32)</sup>.
- **BRAC's help.** BRAC's Migration Programme gives returnees and trafficking survivors airport help at Dhaka through its Migration Welfare Centre, counselling, money or materials to start earning, and legal help <sup>[360](#o360), [N207](#n207)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How does a returnee contact BRAC's Migration Programme?

- **Children's scholarships.** The Welfare Board pays migrants' children scholarships (Tk 27,500 a year at SSC level, Tk 34,000 at HSC) and reserves 0.5% of HSC admission seats for them <sup>[124 (2024)](#o124)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - How does a family apply for the Welfare Board's children's scholarships?
